Named NPCs' "Now" and "Has heard of" move to Playthrough, and the roster is usable at the title screen | One page for both | Under G8 the roster is shown before any meeting, so the journey's state needs a page bound to the journey | Owner, G8 |
| D65 | What Playthrough may say | Status from perception only (here now; alive when last met; away, when the Tarnished passed where they were last met and did not perceive them; fell, when the Tarnished saw it); a story line only for the stage active at the last meeting (EldenNet's `stage_seen`), never a later one; related stories only for met people the stage's own line links; notes only from records; never a next step, a "has something for you" mark, a fate or a count of what is left; its lines and notes are never searched (D21) | Reading flags directly; quest tracks | A flag can hold before its effect is seen (D11); `stage_seen` is what the Tarnished witnessed | SP1–SP9; MEM §2.2 |
| D66 | Pairing a device (G9) | A six-digit code shown in game for two minutes, one use, void after five wrong tries; one random device cookie per device, kept by EldenNet only as a hash with its name and date in `devices.toml` until the player forgets it; Forget revokes it at once and closes its connections, whether `dashboard.lan` is on or off; `dashboard.lan` off closes the network listener and every device connection; the network listener accepts only device cookies and never grants the session cookie; paired devices are refused `secrets.set`, `secrets.clear` and `providers.update`; this PC (a connection on the loopback listener) never needs a code (Since G17 (a) GraceUI keeps the hashes, in `Game\GraceUI\user\devices.toml` (D84); paired devices are refused every command a space marks as sensitive (D81)) (Later, 2026-11-16, D113: the code is valid only while the pairing screen is open in game) | A host field with no pairing; a code per session; a code shown in this PC's browser too | The owner's G9 words; a key is never typed over the network; a code shown in a browser could be read by another user signed in on the same PC (§7.1) | Owner, G9; §7.2 item 16 |
| D67 | Named bosses in the roster | Characters › Named NPCs lists every named NPC and every named boss with a shipped bio from the start (named invaders among the named NPCs), by the name the game shows at a first meeting, read from the player's install. A boss the game never shows calm before its fight is listed by the name its bar shows and carries a "Boss" tag. An NPC the game can show calm before any fight (persona or not; one profile id across forms, EldenNet §7.8) carries no tag and is never listed by its bar name. Which entries carry the tag is fixed per entry in EldenNet's index, never by the playthrough (D64). Its general profile says only what a first meeting shows: for a tagged boss, what the opening of its fight shows; for every other entry, its calm first meeting; each group (Met, Not met, Seen) is in name order, never grouped or ordered by region, kind or order of play, and nothing is counted per region; on NPC types a named boss appears only as its slot among the named slots, and the boss kind row still names no one (Since EldenNet O46, 2026-11-16: the general profile is the bio's `public.summary`, `public.role` and `subjects`, each reviewed at the opening state when written; a tagged boss, or a named boss of a voiceless kind, has the seen fields only and never converses, so it shows no `subjects`; a voiceless one never speaks, and a tagged boss of a speaking kind keeps its combat lines, EldenNet O42) | Bosses only once their bar has shown (SP4 before); a separate Bosses group; order of play | The owner's answers to EldenNet's roster questions ("bios for every named NPCs and bosses with names", "shipped full roster from beginning"); an order by region or progression would hint at what comes next | Owner, EldenNet O43 as updated; G5, G8 |
| D68 | Memories of other NPCs | A keepsake about another NPC (EldenNet O45) shows on History › Memories, and in the `journal/<id>` projection (and its count in `memories`), only when every event it cites had the Tarnished in its audience, and names that NPC as the game shows them (SP3); otherwise it is shown and counted nowhere | Every keepsake; no keepsake about another NPC | Absence is silent (SP5); what the Tarnished heard is theirs to see (SP7) | EldenNet O45; SP3, SP5, SP7 |
| D69 | The host's shape | `graceui.dll` itself is the host: its `grace-host` module alone holds the overlay with every O25 rule (hudhook 0.9.3 and its MinHook until 2026-11-16, then Dear ImGui 1.93.0's DX12 and Win32 backends and MinHook 1.3.4, D107), the Present hook, the window procedure (with EldenNet's `WM_CHAR` surrogate pairing and IME placement) and the DInput and XInput detours for capture and masking; XInput is hooked in every XInput DLL already loaded at install, found by name, so GraceUI reads no import table of the game's; no second GraceUI module. Supersedes G10 (b)'s split and D56's "links no hudhook" | A hook-free GraceUI module beside the host; a dual mode in which EldenNet hosts when present | One process holds one overlay and one MinHook safely; a framework other mods use must be present without EldenNet; a second module adds a private ABI and a second copy to guard, for no isolation within one process | G15 (a); proposal 0001 §4.1 |
| D70 | Finding GraceUI | The helper (`graceui-sys`: header-only C, with a thin C++ wrapper header) runs on the mod's own worker, never in `DllMain`: `GetModuleHandleW(L"graceui.dll")` and its export; else a scan of loaded modules for the export, the first that answers; else, if the mod allows it, `LoadLibraryExW` of `<folder of eldenring.exe>\GraceUI\graceui.dll`; polled every 250 ms for at most 30 s (EST). Exports: `GraceUI_Version()` (callable from any thread, needs no start) and `GraceUI_RequestApi(major)`. Absence means the mod's UI is off, never an error dialog | Load order or a loader's API; a registry of hosts | Every peer host uses this shape, and it survives renaming and duplicates | G15 (a); proposal 0001 §4.3 |
| D71 | Start and threads | Nothing at load. The first `GraceUI_RequestApi` takes the mutex (D72) and starts one short init thread (the anti-cheat check, the game's window, the hooks), which then exits; GraceUI then runs on the present thread, its window procedure and the input detours, plus the server's threads (D81). Mods pull commands, subscriptions, panel input and host events with `poll` on their own threads, from a bounded queue per mod with an optional event handle; `publish` and `submit` copy on the caller's thread into a bounded ring and a triple buffer. An opt-in `on_present` callback binds invariant 10 with a budget (0.5 ms p99 per mod, EST) and is suspended for the session, with one notice, after 120 frames over it. No call posts work to the game's thread (Amended by D133, 2026-11-16: the anti-cheat check runs in the first request, on the mod's thread, before any table is returned and before the init thread starts) (Amended by D212, 2026-11-22: the optional event handle is built, in ABI 1.5, as `wake_event`) | Calling mods back on the present thread; a post to the game's thread | Third-party code stays off the present thread by default; one frame of latency is invisible for menus and HUD text; GraceUI calls no game code | G15 (a); proposal 0001 §4.4 |
| D72 | Single instance | A per-process named mutex (`Local\GraceUI.Host.<pid>`), taken once-guarded in the first `GraceUI_RequestApi` on the calling thread, before any table is returned; a second copy loaded from another folder installs no hook, answers every request with null from its first, idles, and is named by its relative name in one notice (invariant 18) | The first copy by load order; copies negotiating between themselves | A second hooking copy is a second MinHook; bundled copies fight file by file in mod managers | G15 (a); proposal 0001 §4.4, §4.11 |
| D73 | ABI rules | Plain C, default x64 packing; one append-only table per major (`GraceUiApi1`: its size, its ABI minor and GraceUI's version, then `has_cap`, `connect`, `disconnect`, `register_pages`, `publish`, `poll`, `answer`, `register_texture`, `register_face`, `glyph_table`, `create_panel`, `submit`, `show_panel`, `hide_panel`, `request_input`, `release_input`, `register_hotkey`, `notice`, `set_world_state`, `log`); no C++ vtables, Rust types, STL, exceptions, panics or heap ownership across it; every struct a caller passes starts with its size, and the host reads the smaller of the two sizes, defaults the rest and refuses one below the 1.0 size; strings UTF-8 with a length, copied before the call returns; a user pointer on every callback; an SPDX MIT line in every public header. `connect` answers `ok`, `too_old` (with both versions, a notice naming the mod and one log line), `duplicate_id`, `inactive` (with the reason) or `refused`; after `inactive` or `render_dead`, calls do nothing | Rust types or C++ interfaces across the boundary | What every peer host does; an explicit too-old answer avoids the silence the Skyrizz session met on two hosts | Proposal 0001 §4.3, §4.15 |
| D74 | Versions and support | One compatibility rule, written once in the header: the same major, and a minor at least the one built against, or slots checked by size; a new major is a new table from the same export, each major served for at least two minor releases and 12 months after the next (EST); `graceui.toml` and the guide's feature table give the "added in" version of every slot and capability; from GraceUI 1.0 its version is its own semantic version (amending D37 from then on) | A new export per major; no stated window | A public ABI is a promise mods must be able to read; one host name per process | Proposal 0001 §4.3 |
| D75 | Spaces | A space is an id (`[a-z0-9-]{3,32}`, unique, refused if taken), a display name, a version, the GraceUI floor it needs, the views it wants (`game`, `browser`), its rail of categories and pages, and a settings description in §6.9's shape; page data is handed over as UTF-8 JSON bytes at `register_pages`, compiled in or from the mod's own folder; GraceUI never reads or writes another mod's files; each space validates its own commands (invariant 3) | GraceUI reading mods' folders | A mod keeps its files where it chooses; one validating entry point per space | Proposal 0001 §4.2 |
| D76 | Panels and draw lists | Five layers (`hud` < `panel` < `menu` < `modal` < `notice`), with an anchor, bounds and whether a panel may take input; panels start hidden. Draw lists in the default `ImDrawVert` layout (position f32×2, UV f32×2, colour RGBA8) with u16 indices relative to each command's vertex range, from any producer: GraceUI's C++ kit (`grace-draw`, `grace-view`, glyphs from the shared atlas) or a consumer's headless Dear ImGui with no backend and no hooks; a build that overrides either converts first. D56's checker checks every list (counts, indices, clip rectangles, texture ids, the atlas generation); a submission over 256 KiB of vertices or 64 commands is refused and logged (EST). Textures registered once, in the mod's registration window and within its budget (invariant 11) | HTML or a web view in game; a shared ImGui context | An upload during play needs a copy and a fence the present thread must not wait on (invariants 10 and 11; hudhook 0.9.3's leaked buffer, the first reason, lapsed with it on 2026-11-16), so G1's reasons hold for every mod; panels let a mod draw anything without sharing a context, an allocator or a C++ type | G16 (a); proposal 0001 §4.2 |
| D77 | Input and hotkeys | One input holder in the process at a time (the menu, a panel that takes input, a modal); `request_input` answered at once: `granted`, `refused` naming the holder, or `not_now` with the reason (danger, a game menu open, not ready); nothing deposes the holder or queues behind it, and a refused mod may ask to be told when input is free. The one close routine ends any holder, masks held keys until release and tells the holder why. Hotkeys belong to the framework: a named action with a default key and modifiers or a pad chord; a taken default registers unbound, named on GraceUI's Hotkeys page; hotkeys stand aside while any holder has input. The player's exit, a second menu-key press within 2 s of "‹Mod› has the controls" or Escape held 1 s (EST, S4 checks both), ends any holder and is never captured (invariant 17) | Last asker wins; an Ok that only queues | Silent deposing, a true that does nothing and an Ok that only queues are the failures the Skyrizz session met; first holder wins is the rule a player can predict | G15 (a); proposal 0001 §4.5 |
| D78 | Game awareness | Read-only kept. Each space may close and refuse its own views; any consumer may register as a world-state provider (danger, loading, a game menu or text entry open, the title screen, multiplayer, an anti-cheat hit), and every claim is accepted and combined: any provider's danger closes every view and cancels confirmations (invariant 9), a view opens only when every provider allows it or at the title, and any provider's anti-cheat flag closes the server. Without a provider views open at any time and there is no danger close, as the guide says; capture still masks game input while a holder has it. The Compatibility page lists each provider and what it last reported. EldenNet is a provider when present, publishing only these flags | GraceUI reading game state; one provider chosen by load order | Reading game state would need EldenNet's game-access layer, its patch work and O48's checks; a provider gives every mod EldenNet's safety when EldenNet runs | Proposal 0001 §4.6 |
| D79 | The menu's shape | One menu on one key (`hotkeys.menu`, F5 by default, GraceUI's own setting, D84), with GraceUI's frame (rail, top bar, search, key hints); each space brings its own rail; with one space the menu is that space, as designed; from two, a space switcher heads the rail (LB/RB with a modifier, or Ctrl+PageUp/PageDown, EST); GraceUI's own space at the rail's foot (Menu, Hotkeys, Browser view, Installed mods, Compatibility, About); search across every space, each result badged with its space; mods' panels stay outside the menu, on their layers | A menu and a key per mod | One capture owner and one close routine keep input safe (invariants 7 to 9); players with EldenNet alone see no change | Proposal 0001 §4.7 |
| D80 | Theme | Grace is the one look of every space's pages, in both views, from one token file (invariant 13); a space ships no tokens in v1, and a per-space accent token checked for contrast is a later seam; panels look as their mods choose; the shared atlas holds Grace's faces, EldenNet's and at most two for each other mod connected before warm-up (`register_face`, EST), within D29's limits; a rebuild on a scale change raises the atlas's generation | A theme per space | One look and one contrast check for page data; the one atlas and the rule against uploads after load (invariant 11; hudhook's upload leak until 2026-11-16) bound fonts either way | Proposal 0001 §4.8 |
| D81 | Isolation and the gate clause | One origin: no mod supplies HTML, script or CSS, so a space's pages in the browser are its page data drawn by our components, and its strings are always text. Every frame carries its space; subscriptions and commands stay within their space, and limits apply per connection and per space; a space marks its sensitive commands, which paired devices are refused (EldenNet's `secrets.set`, `secrets.clear`, `providers.update`, G9). §7.2 item 1's gate clause kept as four rules: EldenNet registers its space for the browser view only once its gate and safety steps pass, and withdraws it on T10's anti-cheat hit; GraceUI opens no socket after its own anti-cheat hit; a later hit (GraceUI's module re-check every 60 s while the server runs, or any provider's flag) closes the server for every space for the session; an unknown game build binds EldenNet's space alone | Mods' own web code; an origin per mod | One page source and one set of server checks for every mod; in process there is no isolation, so GraceUI's checks guard against mistakes, never a hostile DLL (§7.1) | G17 (a); proposal 0001 §4.9 |
| D82 | EldenNet's pages | EldenNet's space (its categories, pages, settings description, help texts and Home cards), its fixtures, its spoiler, roster and Playthrough goldens, SP1–SP9, its halves of PV1–PV8, D61 and §4's EldenNet content live in EldenNet's repository (EldenNet's PAGES), compiled into `eldennet.dll` and registered at its start. GraceUI keeps the frame and page parts (§4.2), search mechanics (§4.3), the settings row (§4.7), the components, the theme, PV3, PV6 and PV7 as framework rules, and a demo space with invented names and our own words for its own tests and harness; GraceUI's page lint and parity tests run on EldenNet's pages in EldenNet's CI, against a pinned GraceUI | EldenNet's pages bundled in GraceUI's release as its first user's space (G20 (b)) | The owner's "the interactive UI should be part of EldenNet and not GraceUI" (G13's later change); the spoiler rules are EldenNet's domain; a framework must not carry one user's content and its spoiler review | G20 (a), 2026-11-16; proposal 0001 §4.10 |
| D83 | Packaging | `Game\GraceUI\` is the only copy (D51), with `graceui.toml` listing every file's size and hash (D52); a mod states its floor and requires GraceUI, never carrying a private copy; EldenNet carries the release current at its date (D53) in a staging folder, and its launcher, before the game starts, installs it only where none or an older one is installed, never over a newer one, verifying the hashes; an older major is replaced only while the bundled release still serves it (D74), otherwise the launcher explains in E08's manner, naming the mods GraceUI's last log shows connecting on it, and installs only when the player confirms; a player updates by copying a newer release over the folder (G2) | Mods bundling GraceUI; EldenNet copying over whatever is there | Bundled copies fight file by file in mod managers, and a second hooking copy is a second MinHook; the launcher runs before the game, without a running DLL's lock | Proposal 0001 §4.11 |
| D84 | GraceUI's own files | `Game\GraceUI\user\` holds GraceUI's settings, paired devices and scrubbed log: never in a release, outside `graceui.toml`'s hash list, kept when a release is copied over (amends D51 and D19). Its preferences (the menu key, the one `ui.scale` of G6, which EldenNet's text follows, the theme variant, hotkeys, the server) are GraceUI's, migrated out of EldenNet's `settings.toml` | Keeping them in EldenNet's files | A framework's settings cannot live in one consumer's files | Proposal 0001 §4.11 |
| D85 | Docs and harnesses | A modders' guide in `docs/modders/` (the space and page format, every component, the settings description, the protocol, the C ABI and its feature table, panels and "your own Dear ImGui", input and focus, threads and budgets, secrets, versions, distribution, troubleshooting, a bug-report checklist, what MIT allows, how GraceUI is built); three example mods of our own with invented content (a C mod with page data only, a C++ mod with topics and commands, a C++ mod drawing a panel with its own headless Dear ImGui; a Rust consumer of the C ABI may come later, D109); `grace-dev` as a fake host for any space; `grace-harness` (Windows, Direct3D 12, WARP in CI: LIKELY, to be confirmed); a C mock of the ABI; the log's first line names the version, ABI and protocol | No public docs or samples | What Magelight and Meridian offer and what Skyrizz had to build itself | Proposal 0001 §4.12 |
| D86 | Other overlays | Documented: an overlay drawing after GraceUI at Present covers it, and GraceUI cannot draw under the game's own UI; a log warning when Present's first bytes already hold a jump at install (SPECULATION, as a cue only); rows in the compatibility matrix | Detecting and refusing other overlays | A second hooking overlay is outside GraceUI's control under any answer | G15 (a); proposal 0001 §4.1 |
| D87 | Public face | EldenNet's channels (O22: GitHub and a Nexus mirror), with GraceUI's own page; a true AI-assisted disclosure in the README, the guide and every release page (a human-owned design; code, text and docs written with AI assistance and reviewed by hand; no AI-generated images; the tests that guard it); no presence in me3's spaces, with a note that a player may list GraceUI as a native in their own me3 profile; file hashes on every release page | Saying nothing about AI | An honest statement is what the middle ground asks for; unlicensed look-alike "mod menu" repositories exist | Proposal 0001 §4.14 |
| D88 | EldenNet without GraceUI, and anti-cheat | EldenNet's launcher checks GraceUI before start and explains what is missing; in the process, a failed connect leaves EldenNet headless at L2 with a log line, as ARCH §3.2's overlay-error row. On an anti-cheat hit GraceUI installs its hooks as EldenNet's banner path does (capture never on, the input detours pure pass-through), draws notices only (its own, and consumers' `notice`, callable after `connect` answers `inactive (anti-cheat)`), and serves no menu, panel, input or socket for the session. Replaces G2's and D54's "EldenNet keeps playing whatever it finds" | Hooking nothing on a hit, so that EldenNet's banner gives way to the launcher's next-run hint | Under G15 (a) EldenNet cannot draw even its banner without GraceUI; the decided L1 banner stays what players see | G15 (a); proposal 0001 §4.1 |
| D89 | Trails | A trail is the places where the Tarnished perceived a profiled NPC, in order, with the stage then; never a position the Tarnished did not perceive | Tracking positions read from the game | Elden Ring's NPCs do not wander, and EldenNet sees only the characters loaded near the player; perception is SP7's rule | Proposal 0002 §4 |
| D90 | Where masking happens | In EldenNet's core. Every base shows only what the player's own game map shows: the game's tiles in the variant for the map pieces held, so no unrevealed tile is held, drawn or sent anywhere; the layers keep the reached and seen rules and are masked before any projection | Masking in the views; one whole image masked at draw time (the first G26 (a), withdrawn) | The game reveals its map by map piece, so invariant 1 holds whole | G21 (a), G26 (a); proposal 0002 §14.3 |
| D91 | The map panel | A panel kind of the framework: a base (an image a mod hands over, or a vector drawing), layers of marks, lines, areas and labels, a tooltip and a detail pane from page data, zoom and a timeline; one per page; GraceUI draws it and never reads the game | A map drawn by EldenNet's page alone | Both views from one source; a panel kind any mod can use once the framework opens (G18) | G16 (a); proposal 0002 §2 |
| D92 | The Map page's place | Its own category, Map, between Characters and History in EldenNet's rail; opened from the menu, in the browser, or by the map key when its setting is on (G25). Which repository holds the page follows G20. *Later (2026-11-16): G20 (a): EldenNet's, its PAGES §4.2.1; the map's design stays in §4.13* | A page under Characters or History | The map joins the people of Characters and the records of History on one surface | Proposal 0002 §5 |
| D93 | The fit check | A check of the tile set's structure: each tile 256 pixels, the grids 41, 31 and 9 tiles a side, every variant the game's masks name present; a failure draws the drawn base with a one-line notice saying why; never a mismatched image under the layers | The image's size and aspect against the vanilla map's (the proposal's first form) | The game's map is a set of tiles at three zoom levels, revealed by map piece (EldenNet WHY§61, §72, §73, §76, §77) | G21 (a); proposal 0002 §14.3 |
| D94 | The image cache | The composed tiles, in EldenNet's user folder on the player's PC, keyed by the game's version, the source files' hashes and the pieces held; never uploaded, logged or put in a report | Composing at every start; a cache in GraceUI's folder | Composed once per set of pieces; the player's map never leaves their PC except to their own views (PV1–PV8) | G21 (a); proposal 0002 §3, §14.3 |
| D95 | Settings › Map | Base map (your game's map, GraceUI's drawn base, none); The map key opens (the game's map by default, or GraceUI's map); glow (full, soft, off); brighten new things once (off under reduced motion); the layers shown when the map opens; Lore notes by AI (on by default). Each setting has its one home here (invariant 12) | Map settings spread over other pages | One home for every setting | G21 (a), G22 (a), G23 (c), G25 (a); proposal 0002 §3, §6–§8 |
| D96 | The journey timeline | Replays the playthrough from EldenNet's event log; dragged back, the map shows what was reached, who was met and what had changed at that play time; never past now | A live map only | The event log already holds the journey; a replay that stops at now shows nothing not yet reached (SP5) | Proposal 0002 §4 |
| D97 | The drawn base | Exact (the owner, 2026-11-14): built on the player's PC from their own game and cached locally: its areas from the game's area image, its placements by the game's own transforms and conversion table, its shape from the player's own map or, where that does not fit, from the game's map and terrain data; no shape is shipped or committed; not offered if no exact source exists | A shipped, approximate shape of our own | The owner's requirement on every base ("the exact shape of the map, it's parts … accurate placements"); an exact shape traced from the game's map is a copy of its art, so it is built where the game is | Owner, 2026-11-14 (G21's note); proposal 0002 §3, §14.2 |
| D98 | Terrain | Heights built locally with the drawn base and cached, from the game's ground models at the 1 km level, the expansion's land that other models make filled from its far view, and the underground from its maps' own pieces, its floors told from ceilings by their facing and shown as the topmost walkable floor reachable from where the map's characters stand (EldenNet WHY§90, WHY§92, WHY§94); a 3D relief in the browser (tilt, rotate, zoom, with the layers on the terrain); in game a relief image composed with the map's texture and uploaded with it (G26); our own terrain sprites and the tile-grid schematic ship as the fallback | A local 2D relief; our sprites only | The owner's idea of a 3D map read from the game, with nothing of the game shipped; the browser has room for 3D, the in-game view keeps to the map's uploads | G27 (a); proposal 0002 §3 |
| D99 | The Map before 1.1 | Until 1.1 the Map category is a later-version entry in the rail between Characters and History, tagged "WIP" with "1.1" in its tooltip and accessible name, opening a statement page with no controls; Settings › Map joins with the page | Absent until 1.1 | Principle 8 (G7): a planned later-version feature is stated, never faked and never hidden | Fold, 2026-11-15; G7, G24 (a) |
| D100 | Faults inside GraceUI | An exception in GraceUI's own code never crosses the boundary: an ABI entry returns an error code (D110); an exception in the menu's drawing turns the menu off for the session, releases capture and shows one notice, while panels, notices and the browser view keep working; a fault while the server handles a connection closes that connection, and a second stops the server for the session | One fault stopping every surface | Consumers' surfaces should outlive a fault in the menu, as EldenNet's did under D54 | Fold, 2026-11-15; §2.3, §2.8 |
| D101 | GraceUI's keys | The settings D84 moves keep their names (`hotkeys.menu`, `ui.scale`, `ui.menu.*`, `dashboard.*`), so the migration is a move, not a rename; proposal 0001's `menu.key` is `hotkeys.menu` | Renaming them under a `graceui.` prefix | One name per setting in every document, the README and the settings description | Fold, 2026-11-15; D84 |
| D102 | GraceUI's published hook list | GraceUI publishes the functions it hooks (the swap chain's `Present`, `Present1`, `ResizeBuffers` and `ResizeBuffers1`, the command queue's `ExecuteCommandLists`, the game's window procedure, DirectInput's `GetDeviceState`, XInput's `XInputGetState`) in its guide and with each release, append-only; no consumer hooks one of them; CI checks `grace-host`'s targets against it, and EldenNet checks its own against it | A ban on every hook library in a consumer (the fold's first wording) | What must have one owner is each hooked function (EldenNet WHY§86); other functions are not GraceUI's to forbid | EldenNet O65 (2026-11-15) |
| D103 | Plugins on the Content packs page | From 1.1 the page lists EldenNet's plugins (name, version, author, licence, summary, state, order, conflicts, check results), with their switches, order and Remove in both views and, in the browser only, a drop zone with Add file, this PC only and never for code, the file sent by §7.2 item 18's route; "no hub" stands (Superseded the same day by D104: plugins have a page of their own) | A page of their own | Plugins are content layers, which this page already shows | EldenNet O66, O67; its proposal 0001 §5.6, §6.2; G28 |
| D104 | The Plugins page | A page of its own at the foot, `plugins`, from 1.1: a row per plugin with its On/Off switch and, for a code plugin, Cluster or Detached; its state and its clashes or failed checks in words; order for content plugins; the drop zone in the browser; Content packs keeps EldenNet's own content and the overrides, read-only | Plugins on Content packs (D103) | Code plugins are not content, and the owner designed the rows for a plugin page of their own | EldenNet O70 (2026-11-15) |
| D105 | The plugins-stopped pop-up | EldenNet's modal, drawn by GraceUI: "Plugins stopped: <plugin> crashed" (or stopped responding, or used too much memory), the plugin's title in `rot` and bold, how sure the cause is, its version, which plugins stopped and which run on; Details and Copy report; the X at the top right (plugins stay off, the choices stay on the Plugins page) and four buttons: Restart plugins, Restart plugins without <plugin>, Restart plugins with <plugin> detached, Ignore (no plugins). In the browser at once; in game a notice first, the modal only at a safe moment (invariant 9); on EldenNet's launcher at the next start if no choice was made | A notice alone | The owner's design; colour is never the only signal (§3.12) | EldenNet O70; its proposal 0001 §13.6 |
| D106 | C++ for the in-game parts | GraceUI's in-game parts are C++20 (EldenNet O60): each part of §2.2 a CMake static library target of the same name in `src/<module>/`, laid out as EldenNet's modules are; `graceui.dll` a DLL target; `grace-dev` and `grace-harness` C++ programs. Built as EldenNet's are: clang-cl and lld-link (LLVM, pinned) against the MSVC CRT and Windows SDK that xwin downloads (pinned), static CRT, reproducible; a MinGW-w64 build whose Windows tests run under Wine; Linux builds for every portable test. Third-party sources vendored with their licences or fetched at configure time pinned by SHA-256, credited in `NOTICE` and checked in CI against an allowlist, the projects EldenNet O47 and O48 exclude refused by name; JSON through nlohmann/json 3.11.3; tests on doctest 2.4.11 under CTest, with AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer on the Linux runs, ThreadSanitizer for the threaded parts and libFuzzer for every parser; `clang-tidy` with a checked-in configuration and warnings as errors. The browser view stays TypeScript (G3), and the tools that generate code are Python | Rust crates and Cargo, as first designed | EldenNet O60 settled C++ for EldenNet and GraceUI's in-game parts; one toolchain and one set of checks with EldenNet's engine, which is built this way already | EldenNet O60, O84; roadmap S8.1.e |
| D107 | The overlay in the host | Dear ImGui 1.93.0's core with its DX12 and Win32 backends, and MinHook 1.3.4 with its Hacker Disassembler Engine, vendored once at their pinned versions with their licence files (credited in `NOTICE`) and compiled only into `grace-host` (invariant 16); the hooks installed once and never removed, and `graceui.dll` never unloaded, while the game runs; the Win32 backend built without its gamepad code, so the host's XInput detour is the only pad reader (§5.4); the one atlas baked at warm-up and never grown during play (§3.16, invariant 11); a consumer may compile Dear ImGui's core at the same version with no backend (D76). hudhook's own rules (its `dx12` feature, `hudhook::imgui`, `hudhook::mh`, its eject path, `imgui-tables-api`) lapse; the hooked functions stay D102's list | hudhook 0.9.3, as G15 (a) first took it | The owner's choice once the in-game parts became C++ (EldenNet O25 as changed, G15's later change): the same one hook owner and one MinHook in the process; both licences read on 2026-11-16, with no AI clause | Owner, 2026-11-16 (EldenNet O25; G15) |
| D108 | Protocol types and tokens | `grace-protocol`'s envelope, payloads, commands and results are one schema, from which a Python tool generates the C++ types and the web app's TypeScript types, with a test that fails CI on drift; the token build step, also Python, writes CSS custom properties and a C++ header from `tokens/grace.toml` | C++ types as the source, with TypeScript generated from them; hand-written types in each language | Two languages from one source, as the Rust design had; Python is already the repositories' tool language (EldenNet O60) | §6.1, §3.15 |
| D109 | The ABI's sources | `graceui.h` (plain C, SPDX MIT) is `grace-abi`'s source and the public header, compiled as C and as C++ in CI with `static_assert`s on every struct's size and field offsets, and compared with the last release's header (append only); `graceui-sys` is the header-only C helper with a thin C++ wrapper header; the example mods are C and C++, and a Rust consumer of the C ABI may come later (D85) | A header generated from C++ declarations | The ABI was always plain C (D73); a header written as the source has nothing to drift from | §2.2, §2.9; D73 |
| D110 | Exceptions and raw pointers | No exception crosses the C ABI: every entry is `noexcept` with a catch-all guard that returns an error code and writes one short line to GraceUI's scrubbed log, and a consumer's callback must not throw across it; D100's policy stands. Raw pointers from callers, `reinterpret_cast` and the hook code appear only in the DLL's own `abi` files and `grace-host`'s `abi`, `hooks` and `draw` files, each with a comment saying why it is safe, checked in CI by a grep and a `clang-tidy` rule | Exceptions across the boundary; no confinement | The C++ form of the Rust design's caught panics and confined `unsafe`: a fault stays inside GraceUI, and the code that can corrupt memory sits in a few reviewed files | §2.3, §2.9; EldenNet ARCH §2.4 |
| D111 | HTTP connections | WebSockets stay at most two at once (§6.8); plain HTTP connections have a cap of their own, 16 on the loopback listener and 8 on the network listener (EST), each answered with `Connection: close`; a connection that has not sent a complete head within 5 s of being accepted is closed, an absolute deadline, not one per read. Corrects §6.8, which capped every connection at two | One cap of two for every connection, as §6.8 first had it; a timeout per read | Browsers open several connections to one host, and Chrome opens idle ones ahead of need that send nothing, so a cap of two would stall a page load; a timeout per read is what a Slowloris attack defeats | SRV §1, §5.2; G30 |
| D112 | One address shown | Settings › Browser view, Home's Status card, F11 and EldenNet's launcher show `http://127.0.0.1:PORT` only; a top-level navigation whose `Host` is `localhost:PORT` or `[::1]:PORT` is redirected to the same path on `http://127.0.0.1:PORT`, its `Location` written from the listed file's own name (or `/` for a path not listed), so the dashboard has one origin and one cookie jar; `localhost:8642` and `[::1]:8642` still work when typed (§7.2 items 3, 14 and 23). Amends D58 and D60 | Showing and serving the three loopback names alike | `127.0.0.1`, `localhost` and `[::1]` are three origins with three cookie jars, and browsers may try `::1` first for `localhost`, so where another program holds `[::1]:PORT`, "localhost" reaches that program; a path from our own list keeps §7.2 item 19's rule that nothing of the request is written into a header | SRV §7; D58, D60 |
| D113 | The pairing code's window and the firewall prompt | The six-digit code is valid only while the pairing screen is open in game, beside D66's two minutes, single use and five wrong tries; before `dashboard.lan` is first turned on, the game explains Windows' firewall prompt: allowing needs an administrator, and dismissing it leaves a block rule, after which other devices cannot connect (§7.2 item 25) | The code valid for its two minutes whatever the player is doing; no word on the prompt | A six-digit code is a million guesses, so its life should be no longer than the player's own act of pairing; a dismissed prompt fails silently for other devices, and its rule covers the game's executable, not the port | SRV §6, §7; G9, D66 |
| D114 | No secure-context features | The browser view depends on no feature limited to secure contexts (service workers, `crypto.subtle`), so the same web app works on a paired device; the one exception is the upload (§7.2 item 18), which only the loopback listener takes, a secure context, so its SHA-256 uses `crypto.subtle` there (§7.2 item 25) | Service workers for caching; `crypto.subtle` for hashing | A page on a private address over plain HTTP, as `dashboard.lan` serves it, is not a secure context, so those features are missing there | SRV §6; G9 |
| D115 | GraceUI's own settings, on its own pages | GraceUI's keys (`hotkeys.menu`, `ui.scale`, `ui.menu.theme` and the menu's own options, `dashboard.*`) are homed on the pages of GraceUI's own space at the rail's foot (D79): Menu (`ui.scale`, `ui.menu.*`), Hotkeys (`hotkeys.menu`, and every registered hotkey listed with its space for its conflicts, each changed on its own space's page; D77) and Browser view (`dashboard.*`, the address, browsers connected, pairing and paired devices); their commands go through GraceUI's own space. A space's page may carry a link to one of them where a player would look (EldenNet's Text and subtitles to the size, its Hotkeys to the menu key): navigation only, no command crosses spaces. A setting that arranges or shows a space's own pages is that space's: EldenNet's Home cards and World page are `ui.home_cards` and `ui.show_world` in EldenNet's settings. Earlier entries' Settings › Menu, Hotkeys and Browser view name these pages (Amended by D208, 2026-11-22: every mod's hotkey is changed on GraceUI's Hotkeys page, not on its own space's, and a space's page keeps no key settings of its own; G37) | Homes on EldenNet's pages, as listed until now | §6.9's lint (a key's home is a page of its own space) and D81 (commands stay within their space) hold, and every mod finds GraceUI's settings in one place | G20 (a); EldenNet's WHY§107 and roadmap S7.8.e |
| D116 | Finding the present queue | GraceUI draws on the direct queue the game presents with, found in this order, each way sound by the contract it rests on: (1) the swap chain's own answer to `GetDevice` for `ID3D12CommandQueue`, where the runtime keeps the queue it was created with as the swap chain's device (Wine 9.0's and vkd3d-proton's swap chains answer with the device instead, by their source and a probe of ours); (2) the queue the game names for every buffer in `ResizeBuffers1`, which replaces any other answer; (3) a direct queue the runtime executes inside the original `Present` of that swap chain, on its thread; (4) the only direct queue of the swap chain's device seen executing command lists, once a whole frame has been watched and it executed in the last one. With two or more direct queues and no other answer, nothing is drawn, with one log line, until the game names one (Amended by D153, 2026-11-16: the fourth way names a candidate only, and its reference is taken inside a call of its own to `ExecuteCommandLists`; a queue not seen executing for 120 presents (EST) no longer counts) | The first queue seen executing, as many overlays take it; an offset into the swap chain's private object; a hook on the factory's `CreateSwapChain*` | Work on another queue races the present: a torn or missing overlay. The factory is not in D102's list, and GraceUI may start after the swap chain exists. Way 4 assumes that a present queue executes command lists of its own, which is legal not to; S4 records which way answers in the game, on Windows and on Proton | S10.4.a; the harness's two-queues checks |
| D117 | The atlas in Dear ImGui 1.93 | Dear ImGui's static mode: the renderer flag `ImGuiBackendFlags_RendererHasTextures` is cleared after the DX12 backend's `Init`, so `ImFontAtlasBuildMain` bakes every glyph range at warm-up and Dear ImGui locks the atlas during every frame (no new glyph and no new size: the nearest bake is scaled). GraceUI uploads the RGBA atlas itself, once, on the present queue, and polls its fence on later frames, drawing nothing until it has passed; each frame's draw data reaches the backend with `Textures = nullptr`. Dear ImGui's obsolete functions are compiled out (Amended by D148, 2026-11-16: the bake runs on GraceUI's own thread, which frees the CPU copy once the upload buffer holds it) | The dynamic atlas through the backend's texture path; per-font locks (`ImFontFlags_LockBakedSizes`, `ImFontFlags_NoLoadGlyphs`, both marked internal) | The backend's texture path waits for the GPU on the calling thread (`WaitForSingleObject(..., INFINITE)` in `ImGui_ImplDX12_UpdateTexture`), and a dynamic atlas uploads during play (invariants 10 and 11); the static mode is Dear ImGui's own lock | §3.16; D107 |
| D118 | Back buffers and swap chains | GraceUI holds no reference to a back buffer between frames: each buffer's render-target view is made at its first use, with a reference held only for that call, and forgotten when `ResizeBuffers` or `ResizeBuffers1` is about to replace the buffers (an atomic mark the next frame takes, set from any thread) and when the swap chain ends. Each swap chain is tagged at its first `Present` with a COM object of GraceUI's, set as private data (`SetPrivateDataInterface`): DXGI releases it when the swap chain ends, which unbinds it before its memory can be reused, so a new swap chain at the same address is bound afresh. One Direct3D 12 swap chain on a window of the process is bound at a time: another passes through untouched and untagged, and is looked at again once the bound one ends; one that is not Direct3D 12, or not on a window of the process, passes through for good | References held between frames and released in `ResizeBuffers` | A reference held between frames keeps a released swap chain alive, and the game's next swap chain on the same window then fails (one flip-model swap chain per window); `ResizeBuffers` fails while one is held | The harness's references and new-swap-chain checks |
| D119 | Frames in flight | Four frame slots (EST), each a command allocator and a command list, in step with the DX12 backend's own frame resources, whose index advances on each `RenderDrawData`; a slot whose fence has not passed is skipped and counted, never waited for; one fence, signalled on the present queue, for every slot and the atlas (Amended by D153, 2026-11-16: after the present queue changes, nothing of GraceUI's goes to the new one until its last work on the old one has passed, since `Signal` sets the one fence's value) | One slot per back buffer | The backend counts its own calls, not back buffers, so slots tied to buffers would fall out of step with it on a skipped frame; four covers DXGI's default frame latency of three, plus one | Invariant 10 |
| D120 | The init thread | The game's window, game-agnostically: the largest visible top-level window of the process that no other window owns, polled every 100 ms for at most 120 s (EST). The targets are read by name from the vtables of a short-lived device (the adapter's one device, the game's if it has made it), direct queue and flip-model swap chain on a never-shown window of GraceUI's own, all released before any hook exists. The five hooks are created in one loop over one table, the one place any hook is made, and enabled together; if one cannot be created, none is enabled and the host stays idle with one log line. The DLL pins itself before the thread starts (Amended by D125 and D127, 2026-11-16: the input half's entries are `GetDeviceState` and `XInputGetState` once per XInput DLL found, under the same all-or-nothing rule, and the window procedure's filter is a subclass made once after the table's hooks are enabled, not a MinHook hook) (Amended by D146 and D147, 2026-11-16: console windows are not taken; the filter moves to the bound swap chain's window when that is another; the init thread stays as GraceUI's own thread; and, by D150, the hooks MinHook enabled before one failed pass everything through) | A window class or title; hooking each function as it is found | No game knowledge (EldenNet's rules); the input half adds its four hooks to the same table | D71, D102, D107 |
| D121 | Failures inside Dear ImGui | `IM_ASSERT` throws an exception of the host's, which the `Present` detour catches: the render path turns itself off for the session with one log line, and the game's frames pass through untouched from then (D100's rule, applied to the whole render path until the menu exists); the assertion's text carries no file name | `assert`, which aborts the game in a debug build and checks nothing in a release one | A fault stays inside GraceUI, and a release build keeps the library's checks | D100, D110 |
| D122 | The Win32 backend in the render half | Initialised for the bound swap chain's window, but its `NewFrame` (mouse, keys, cursor) is the input half's, called while a holder has input; the render half sets the display size to the swap chain's buffer size and the time step from the performance counter itself, so it asks the window nothing on the present thread and draws one to one onto the back buffer. The cursor is never changed (`ImGuiConfigFlags_NoMouseCursorChange`), Dear ImGui writes no file (`IniFilename = nullptr`) and draws nothing of its own (no error tooltip) (Amended by D130, 2026-11-16: its `NewFrame` is not called at all; the input half feeds Dear ImGui itself) | Calling `ImGui_ImplWin32_NewFrame` every frame | Its window calls belong with capture and masking (§5); under Wine each is a call to the server | S10.4.a's input half |
| D123 | Test exports and the build | `GRACEUI_TEST_EXPORTS`, a CMake option off by default, adds `GraceUI_Test_*` (`graceui_test.h`) and the counters behind them, never in a release build (`tools/check_safety.py --exports` checks a DLL's export table). Dear ImGui and MinHook are fetched file by file at their tags' commits, each pinned by SHA-256, and compiled with their warnings off in targets only grace-host links; our own code keeps 0 warnings with `-Wold-style-cast`, so every cast is a named one the check can confine (D110); binaries name their PDB by file name only (`/pdbaltpath`) | Release archives (GitHub's generated archives are not promised to stay byte for byte the same) | Per-file pins are stable and each file is reviewable | D106, D107, D110 |
| D124 | The input half's rules, pure | `grace-input`, a pure module (no Win32, no Dear ImGui, no MinHook; `tools/check_safety.py` refuses each): the input holder (D77) and its one close routine, the player's exit and the 2 s watchdog, the game's view of DirectInput and XInput, the holder's pad view, the window procedure's routing, the character pairing, and the two hand-overs between threads (a one-producer ring and a sequence lock that never blocks); unit-tested on Linux with doctest 2.4.11, fetched pinned for the tests only, under AddressSanitizer, UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer and ThreadSanitizer, and with GCC; a Linux build holds the pure modules only. It holds the host's side of the pad ledger §5.7 and §9.1 give `grace-view`, which will use it rather than a copy | The rules inside `grace-host`, checked only in the harness | They decide what the game sees: on Linux every path, race and timing can be tested, and the harness then checks the Win32 side | S10.4.a's input half; D106; invariant 16 |
| D125 | The window procedure's filter | A subclass of the window D120 finds, made once by the init thread after the table's hooks are enabled, through `SetWindowLongPtrW` or `SetWindowLongPtrA` as the window is Unicode or ANSI, passing on through `CallWindowProc`; never removed. With no holder every message reaches the game but the menu key's, which never does (§5.1). While a holder takes the keyboard, key, character and IME messages are GraceUI's (Alt+F4 still reaches the game), IME messages going to `DefWindowProc` alone so that the system's composition window works; a press that began while a holder had input stays GraceUI's to its release, after the holder ends too (invariant 8), and a release the game saw begin always reaches it; mouse messages and raw input likewise for a holder that takes the mouse. A holder gets a key's messages only from a press it saw begin (the opening press, invariant 8). Focus loss (`WM_KILLFOCUS`, `WM_ACTIVATEAPP`) ends the holder and forgets what was held (Amended by D146, 2026-11-16: a subclass on each window the filter is given, at most four (EST), only the newest active; one it has left passes every message on) | A MinHook detour on the game's window procedure; `SetWindowSubclass`; a `WH_GETMESSAGE` hook | The game's procedure is game code, which GraceUI never patches; `SetWindowSubclass` works only on the window's own thread; a message hook cannot keep a sent message from the game. Keeping the window's character set leaves later subclasses as they would be without GraceUI (an ANSI subclass crashed against a Unicode one in Magelight's reports, ML) | D69, D102, D120 |
| D126 | Characters | The filter pairs a Unicode window's UTF-16 halves into code points, a lone or misplaced half becoming U+FFFD; joins an ANSI window's lead and trail bytes and decodes them in its keyboard layout's code page, which `WM_INPUTLANGCHANGE` follows; takes an ANSI window's double-byte character whole from `WM_IME_CHAR`; and answers `WM_UNICHAR` while a holder has the keyboard. Dear ImGui's characters are 32-bit (`IMGUI_USE_WCHAR32`), so a code point beyond U+FFFF reaches it whole. A character from a key the holder did not see pressed is dropped; one no key made (an IME's, a posted one) is the holder's (Amended by D153, 2026-11-16: an ANSI window whose code page is UTF-8 has its bytes joined by UTF-8's own rules) | Dear ImGui's own pairing (`AddInputCharacterUTF16`) | A holder takes characters, not UTF-16 halves (§5.6); with 16-bit characters Dear ImGui turns every code point beyond U+FFFF into U+FFFD | §5.6; the harness's characters checks |
| D127 | The input detours | `IDirectInputDevice8W::GetDeviceState`, read from a short-lived keyboard and mouse made through the `DirectInput8Create` of the `dinput8.dll` already loaded (one function for both under Wine; both hooked if they differ); GraceUI never loads `dinput8.dll` itself, since a mod loader may stand in its place. `XInputGetState` in each of `xinput1_4.dll`, `xinput1_3.dll`, `xinput9_1_0.dll`, `xinput1_2.dll` and `xinput1_1.dll` already loaded, each function once. Each DLL hooked is pinned. Every input hook is an entry of D120's one table, under its all-or-nothing rule. A detour calls the original first and rewrites only the outermost call's answer: a keyboard (by `GetCapabilities`) read as 256 bytes, a mouse read as `DIMOUSESTATE` or `DIMOUSESTATE2`; any other device or format passes untouched, logged once. `GetDeviceData`, the ANSI interface and `XInputGetStateEx` are not hooked (D102's list): S4 records whether the game reads them | Loading `dinput8.dll` or an XInput DLL; reading the game's import table | D69's rule; a DLL unloaded under a hook would leave the hook pointing at nothing | D69, D102, D120 |
| D128 | The game's view, during and after a holder | While a holder takes a device, the game reads it at rest: no key, no mouse movement, wheel or button, a pad untouched. When the holder ends, whatever was held stays hidden until it is released (invariant 8): each reader arms its masks at its first read after the close, with what is held then and was held at the read before, or with all that is held if the close was 250 ms ago or less (EST: the closing press may not have been read yet); a holder that did not take a device leaves no mask on it. A pad's triggers and sticks count as held past XInput's own thresholds. XInput's packet number is the pad's own plus one for each grant and each close, so the game reads every change (EldenNet NPC §5.5's "bumped once", made exact); a pad no holder ever took passes bit for bit. The menu key is always hidden | Masking what the last read before the close saw held | The press that ends a holder is often down before any read sees it | Invariant 8; EldenNet NPC §5.5 |
| D129 | The holder's mechanics | One 64-bit word, changed only by compare-and-swap: a request is granted, refused naming the holder (its owner and kind) or not_now (not ready: no overlay frame in the last 2 s, no window filter, or input off; danger; loading; a game menu; an invalid request); the holder asking again for the same kind is told it holds input. The one close routine moves the holder to closing, tells the devices (a count they read), then ends it, so that no reader sees the holder gone without its masks; any thread may run it, one close per holder. The player's exit: the menu key's presses come from the window's messages only (one press seen through two sources would count twice); Escape held is watched in the messages and in DirectInput, each with its own timer, counted from the grant. 2 s without an overlay frame ends the holder. A fault in the filter or a detour turns input off for the session; the render path turning off ends the holder (D100) (Amended by D145, D150 and D151, 2026-11-16: conditions read afresh before and after a grant, never from copies; the render path turning off turns input off; the watchdog hears only frames drawn or with nothing to draw) | A lock; the last asker wins | Nothing waits (invariant 10), and every answer is at once (invariant 17) | D77; invariants 7, 8 and 17 |
| D130 | Feeding Dear ImGui | The window thread queues the holder's keys, characters and mouse (in client pixels) in a ring of 256; on the present thread, before `NewFrame`, the host replays them into Dear ImGui in back-buffer pixels, at most 64 a frame while Dear ImGui's own queue holds fewer than 128 (it takes one change of a key or button a frame), consecutive mouse moves as one; the modifiers come from the holder's own key events; Dear ImGui's queues are reserved at warm-up, so a holder's input allocates nothing during play. At each new holder and at each end, Dear ImGui forgets every key and button. While a holder has the mouse, Dear ImGui draws its own cursor and the OS cursor is confined to the client area, the previous clip put back at the close; the pad's navigation comes from the XInput detour's view of the newest pad, the opening press held back | The Win32 backend's `NewFrame` and window-procedure handler | They call `GetKeyState`, `GetCursorPos`, `TrackMouseEvent` and `SetCapture`, which answer for, or act on, the calling thread, and the present thread need not be the window's; Dear ImGui's `io.Add*` calls are not thread-safe | D122; invariant 10 |
| D131 | ABI 1's first table | `graceui.h`, ABI 1.0 (a preview until G18's 1.1), holds the table's size, its ABI minor, GraceUI's version and a reserved word, then D73's twenty slots in D73's order. A slot whose shape the design names is typed now (`has_cap`, `connect`, `disconnect`, `notice`; textures, faces and the glyph table; panels and draw lists in D76's layout; `request_input` and `release_input`; `register_hotkey`; `set_world_state`; `log`), each taking the mod's handle and a struct that starts with its size, so that its increment adds fields rather than changes the slot; the bridge protocol's four (`register_pages`, `publish`, `poll`, `answer`) are reserved slots (`GraceUiReservedSlot`) that keep their place and get their type when §6's protocol is built. One set of statuses for every slot: ok, too_old, duplicate_id, inactive, refused, not_connected, not_yet, full, fault; `inactive` names anti-cheat, the host failing to start or the render path dead, and `refused` the argument, the id, the name, the caps or too many mods. `connect` checks the strings (an id of 3 to 32 of a-z, 0-9 and '-'; a name of 1 to 64 bytes of UTF-8 text; caps as cap names separated by spaces), then the floor (0 for any), then the id among the mods connected, then room (32 mods, EST); with ok and with inactive the mod is connected and holds a handle (its slot and the slot's generation, so a handle from before a disconnect names nobody), and after inactive every slot but `has_cap`, `disconnect` and, in the notice-only mode, `notice` answers inactive. A too-old mod gets GraceUI's notice naming it and both versions, and one log line (log lines of failed connects at most 16 a session). GraceUI 0.1 provides `has_cap` (`notices`), `connect`, `disconnect` and `notice`; every other slot answers not_connected, inactive or not_yet (Amended by D140, 2026-11-16: S10.4.c provides the textures', faces', panels', input's and providers' slots too, and `has_cap` names their five caps; the bridge protocol's four, `register_hotkey` and `log` still answer not_yet). The rules are `grace-abi`'s, pure and unit-tested on Linux (the mods connected, the notices, the checks on a mod's strings, the anti-cheat check's matching); the DLL's `abi` files copy a mod's struct, the smaller of the two sizes, and its strings, within their bounds, before the rules see them (D110) (Amended by D155, 2026-11-17: `poll` is typed, and `register_hotkey` and `poll`'s hotkey events are provided in 0.1, with the cap `hotkeys`; three of the bridge protocol's four and `log` still answer not_yet) | Declaring only the slots built, in the order the increments build them; typing every slot now, the protocol's four included | D73's order holds and nothing is guessed: a slot the design shapes is typed, and one that waits on §6 keeps its place; structs that start with their size let an increment grow a slot without breaking a mod built against this header | S10.4.b; D73, D74, D109 |
| D132 | The header's check | ABI 1.0's first header is kept as `src/grace-abi/baseline/graceui-abi1-0.h`. `tools/check_safety.py` (check 8) refuses a `graceui.h` that changes or drops a constant (`GRACEUI_ABI1_MINOR` may only rise), a typedef or an export, or that changes a struct otherwise than by adding members at its end, the table's slots among them; a reserved slot may be given its type in its place. `--abi-self-test` shows it refusing each kind of break and letting each kind of growth through, on edits of the header. Every struct's size and every field's offset are `static_assert`ed in C and in C++ by every build, on Linux and with both Windows toolchains, and the two layout programs check `GRACEUI_API1_COVERS`. While ABI 1 is a preview, a deliberate break updates the baseline in the same commit, which says why | A check against the last release's header only, from 1.0 | §2.9 asks for it, and every later header must hold the first one; the layout's numbers catch a compiler or packing that reads the same header otherwise | §2.9; D109 |
| D133 | The anti-cheat check and the notice-only mode | Run once, in the first `GraceUI_RequestApi` of the copy that takes the mutex, on the mod's thread, before any table is returned and before the init thread starts (amending D71), so that `connect`'s first answer already knows it. EldenNet ARCH §5.3's two clauses: a module of the process (`EnumProcessModules` and its base name) whose file name starts with `EasyAntiCheat`, or a parent process (a Toolhelp snapshot of the processes) whose image is `start_protected_game.exe`, ASCII letters compared without their case; a parent that has exited is skipped, and a process number reused since can only err towards a hit; nothing is opened in another process. A hit is the notice-only mode for the session (D88): the hooks are installed as usual; input is off from the start, so every detour and the window filter pass everything to the game, the menu key included, and capture is never on; nothing is drawn but notices; `connect` answers inactive (anti-cheat) with a handle, and `notice` stays callable; GraceUI shows one notice of its own (20 s, EST) and writes one log line. It is not checked again during the session: D81's 60 s re-check and a provider's anti-cheat flag close the server, which comes later. A test build may be told other names to look for (`GraceUI_Test_SetAntiCheatNames`), so that the harness simulates a hit by each clause with no anti-cheat software present | The check on the init thread, as D71 first listed it, with `connect` answering ok until it has run; hooking nothing on a hit (D88 declined it); a module or file named after the anti-cheat in the tests | A mod connects as soon as it holds the table, and an answer that changes later is the silence D73 forbids; the check costs one list of modules and one snapshot of processes; tests should exercise the gathering and the matching alike, with no product's name in any file | D88; EldenNet ARCH §5.3 |
| D134 | Notices in GraceUI 0.1 | A notice is a tone (a gold, ember or rot accent), a key (0 for a new notice; a key replaces the notice showing under it in its place, and an empty text withdraws it), a duration from the call (0 until replaced or withdrawn: a banner) and 1 to 512 bytes of UTF-8 text with no control character but the line feed; 4 of a mod's and 8 of GraceUI's own show at once (EST), 16 in all, beyond which `notice` answers full; a mod's go when it disconnects. Mods' threads write under a spin lock; the present thread reads the newest list through a triple buffer and never waits. GraceUI 0.1 draws them plainly until S10.4.c gives them the Grace look and its faces: boxes 300 px wide at the top right, 12 px from the edges, `ground.2` with a 3 px accent and `ink.hi` text in Dear ImGui's own font, at most 3 lines each and 5 notices, oldest first, above everything else drawn. Warm-up ends with one frame of Dear ImGui's, built and never submitted, which makes the foreground draw list and reserves room for the most a frame of notices draws; Dear ImGui's draw-list compaction is off (`ConfigMemoryCompactTimer` at `FLT_MAX`). GraceUI's own notices last 20 s (EST): a too-old mod, a second copy, the anti-cheat hit (Amended by D141, 2026-11-16: the Grace look, in GraceUI's own face, on the notice layer above the mods' modals) (Amended by D181, 2026-11-20: a mod the player quieted has its notices answered as before but kept on the Mods page, not drawn) | Notices only from S10.4.c; a kind (toast or banner) as a field now | The notice-only mode and the second copy's notice need them now; a duration of 0 is a banner, and a key lets a mod change or withdraw one without a slot of its own; Dear ImGui frees a draw list unused for 60 s of its own time, which leaps forward when frames resume after a quiet spell, so a notice would allocate on the present thread during play (invariant 10) | S10.4.b; D76, D88 |
| D135 | Two copies, and a request made at once | A request made while the first is still running on another thread waits for it (a spin with a yield, on a mod's thread), so every caller gets the same answer. `GraceUI_RequestApi(1)` answers the table in the copy that holds the mutex, whatever its host's state (`connect` then says why it is inactive), and null in an idle copy, for any other major and where the mutex could not be made. At each of its requests the active copy looks through the loaded modules (`EnumProcessModules`, each held by a reference while it is looked at) and names every other one that exports `GraceUI_RequestApi`, once each (at most 8), in one notice (ember, 20 s) and one log line, by its name relative to the game's folder (the folder of the process's executable), or by its file name and the words "outside the game's folder", never a path (PV5). An idle copy, once its own first request is over, asks every other copy for ABI 1's table, so that the active one hears of it at once (Amended by D153, 2026-11-16: a request made while the first is running waits 2 s at most (EST), then answers null; a copy's name too long for its notice keeps its end, after "...") | A block of shared memory between copies; naming only the copies a mod's helper happens to ask | The two exports are all the copies share; an idle copy asks only after its own first request is over, so no two copies wait on each other | D72; invariant 18 |
| D136 | `graceui-sys` and the first example | `graceui_sys.h`, header-only C on kernel32 alone: `graceui_sys_find(flags, timeout_ms, &found)` looks every 250 ms for at most the time it is given (30 s by default, EST), each time three ways: the module named `graceui.dll`; every loaded module that exports `GraceUI_RequestApi`, in load order (a Toolhelp snapshot of the modules, each held by a reference while it is asked); and, with `GRACEUI_SYS_LOAD`, `GraceUI\graceui.dll` in the folder of the process's executable, D70's "folder of eldenring.exe" read game-agnostically, loaded by `LoadLibraryExW` with the DLL-folder and System32 search flags. It calls `GraceUI_RequestApi(1)` on each and says how, and at which look, it found the table. `graceui_sys.hpp` is the thin C++ wrapper (`graceui::Api`, `graceui::Mod`), each call checking that the table covers its slot. The first of D85's examples, `examples/hello-notice` (C, invented), starts a thread in `DllMain`, finds GraceUI with the helper, connects and shows a notice; the harness runs it found by name after polling, by its scan and by path | `psapi`'s module list in the helper; the helper loading GraceUI only by name | Kernel32 alone keeps the helper free of a library's version; the three ways are D70's, and the harness shows each | D70, D85 |
| D137 | Panels and their draw lists in GraceUI 0.1 | A panel's draw lists are in the panel's own pixels (its top-left corner at 0,0), so a mod needs no display size: GraceUI moves them where the anchor puts the panel, x and y measured inwards from the anchor's edges (from the centre, rightwards and downwards, for a centred anchor), and every clip rectangle must lie within the panel's width and height (1 to 16,384 px), so nothing is drawn outside it; GraceUI clips to the display too. 16 panels a mod and 64 in all (EST); hidden at first; gone with their mod. `submit` checks the counts against the bounds before it copies anything (256 KiB of vertices, 256 KiB of indices and 64 commands, EST; a command stride of at least ABI 1.0's 40 bytes), copies on the mod's thread into the panel's back buffer, checks GraceUI's own copy (D76's list, each index within its own command's vertices) and publishes it only if it passes; a refused list leaves the panel its last one and is logged once for each connection of the mod. Each panel's lists are a triple buffer: the writer's buffer is never the reader's, so the writer grows or frees it on its own thread and no retire slot is needed (§2.9's is for a shared snapshot); the present thread takes the newest, moves its vertices in place once to where the panel lies, and lends its arrays to an ImDrawList of its own, one a panel slot made at warm-up, for the one `RenderDrawData` that copies them, taking them back before it returns. Layers in one frame's draw data: the mods' hud, then panel, then GraceUI's own drawing (Dear ImGui's lists: the menu's layer, the test panel in 0.1), then the mods' modal, then the notices and Dear ImGui's cursor for a holder; within a layer, panels in the order of their slots. Nothing is drawn of panels in the notice-only mode (Amended by D152, 2026-11-16: a command stride of at most 256 bytes, `GRACEUI_DRAW_COMMAND_SIZE_MAX`, EST) | A list in the display's pixels (a mod would need the display's size, which no slot gives); `std::atomic<std::shared_ptr>` snapshots with a retire slot; copying each list into GraceUI's own ImDrawList every frame (room for 64 panels' largest lists reserved at warm-up, or allocation during play) | The checker reads only GraceUI's copy, which the mod cannot change while it is read; the present thread allocates and frees nothing (the allocation tests count it) and copies nothing but what the DX12 backend copies anyway. The backend grows its per-frame vertex and index buffers when a frame's lists first exceed them, a Direct3D buffer made on the present thread without waiting and none of GraceUI's heap; S4 measures it in the game | S10.4.c; D76, §2.9; invariant 10 |
| D138 | Textures at load | A mod's registration window runs from its connect to its first `submit` (proposal 0001 §4.2's "before the mod's first submission"); a texture after it answers `too_late`. Its budget, per connection: 8 textures and 8 MiB of RGBA8 pixels (EST); in all 64 textures and 64 MiB (EST); each side at most 4,096 px. The pixels are copied, rows packed, on the mod's thread. The textures registered before warm-up join the atlas's upload, in one command list and one fence; a later mod's are uploaded together at the frame after, on the present queue, if the upload list is free, never waited on; a command naming a texture still on its way is not drawn. The upload counter counts each batch (warm-up's first). A mod's pixels are freed on a mod's thread once the present thread has copied them; once its mod has gone, a texture's GPU objects are released after the fence of the last frame that could draw it, and its slot is freed on a mod's thread | A window closed at warm-up (a mod that connects later could draw nothing of its own); uploads on a thread of GraceUI's (the copy must be on the present queue to be ordered before the frames that draw it) | Invariant 11: an upload during play is a late mod's one batch, never a per-frame cost, and nothing waits; freeing on mods' threads keeps the present thread free of frees (invariant 10) | S10.4.c; D76, D80; invariant 11 |
| D139 | Faces and glyph tables | `register_face` before warm-up only: warm-up seals the table, and a face being written then is late (`too_late`, its data freed by its writer). A mod's two faces (EST), EldenNet's (the id `eldennet`) eight, 24 of mods' in all (EST); a font file of at most 4 MiB whose sfnt table directory lies within it and names the tables a face needs; 6 to 128 px; ranges as pairs from U+0020 to U+10FFFF outside the surrogates, at most 64 pairs and 65,536 code points, none for §3.4's default (Latin, Latin-1, Latin Extended-A, General Punctuation, U+FFFD). The data stays for the session (the atlas reads it). At warm-up GraceUI adds its notices' face first (Dear ImGui's default font), then the mods' in registration order, with Dear ImGui's recoverable errors logged rather than thrown while it adds them, so that a file Dear ImGui cannot parse is left out with one log line; bakes once (D117); and makes each face's glyph table, sorted by code point, the pen at the top of the line as Dear ImGui's, kept for the session. Face ids: GraceUI's own from 1 (the notices' face is 1), the mods' from 16. `glyph_table` answers `not_ready` before warm-up, and the atlas's generation is 1 for the session in 0.1 (Amended by D148, 2026-11-16: within the atlas's room, each face's estimated area counted when it is committed, `full` past it; baked on GraceUI's own thread, and baked again with fewer faces should Dear ImGui still run out of room) (Amended by D167, 2026-11-20: GraceUI's own faces in the atlas are the notices' and, from S11.2.b, the menu's eleven, one for each type role at the effective scale; a bake that runs out of room drops the mods' faces first, then the menu's) | Faces added after warm-up (a second upload of the atlas during play); a mod's broken font failing the whole bake | §3.16 and D80: one atlas, baked once; a mod's error is the mod's, not the overlay's | S10.4.c; §3.16, D80, D117 |

No struct changes. GraceUI 0.1 is not released, so these are ABI 1.0 still: the minor stays 0 and the baseline stays S10.4.b's header, which the header only grows against (D132) | `refused` for a late call; raising the minor before any release | A mod can tell "try later" and "never in this session" from a bad argument; check 8 holds | S10.4.c; D131, D132 |
| D141 | Notices in the Grace look | §3.8's toast: a `ground.2` card with a `rule` border and 4 px corners, the 3 px accent drawn over the border as its left edge, ember's and rot's with their icon (a triangle, a circle, each with a mark of exclamation: D25), `ink.hi` text in Noto Sans Regular 15 on 22 px lines (`ui`), 12 px of padding beside the text and 8 above and below, at most 3 lines and 5 notices, oldest first, at the top right; words wrap as Dear ImGui wraps them. The face is GraceUI's own file, `fonts\NotoSans-Regular.ttf` beside `graceui.dll` (D51), read whole by the init thread so that warm-up reads no file; without it, Dear ImGui's own font on 17 px lines, with one log line naming the file by its relative name.
