| D142 | Input through ABI 1 | `request_input` takes the mod's own panel, made with `GRACEUI_PANEL_TAKES_INPUT` and shown, as a modal holder for a panel on the modal layer and a panel holder otherwise; anything else is `refused` as an argument, and a hidden panel is `not_now (not ready)`. The answer is S10.4.a's holder's, with the holder's mod id (or `graceui` for the menu) and kind. Hiding the holding panel, and its mod's disconnect, end that holder (released), and only that holder; `release_input` ends it if the mod holds input. The menu key's first press while a mod holds input shows GraceUI's own notice, "‹Mod› has the controls. Press F5 again to take them back." (2 s, the player's exit's window), made without formatting on the window procedure's thread. What a holder learns of its own end, and its keys, characters and pointer, reach it through `poll`, which comes with §6's protocol: in 0.1 a panel holder takes the input from the game, and Dear ImGui's cursor is drawn for it (Amended by D153, 2026-11-16: the holder's panel is published and then asked for under the panels' lock, so hiding or removing it on another thread ends the holder) (Amended by D155, 2026-11-17: `poll` exists from 0.1 with hotkey events alone; a holder's own events still come with §6) (Amended by D176, 2026-11-20: they come in ABI 1.1, GraceUI 0.2.1, to a mod that asks for them) | A new slot for a holder's events before §6 | D77's answers and the player's exit need no event; D73's order keeps `poll` for them | S10.4.c; D77, D129 |
| D143 | World-state providers in GraceUI 0.1 | Any connected mod's `set_world_state` replaces its own claims; every provider's claims are combined (or-ed); a provider's go when it disconnects. Danger or loading ends the input holder and makes `request_input` answer `not_now` (danger, loading); a game menu makes it answer `not_now (game menu)`; an anti-cheat flag is logged once (the browser view, D81, comes later); the title screen and multiplayer are kept for the menu, which comes later. Danger ends the view that holds input; panels that hold none (a mod's hud) stay, their mods' to hide (Amended by D145, 2026-11-16: the input half reads the combined claims where the providers write them, and the last provider to report decides) | Hiding every panel on danger | Invariant 9 closes views; a hud panel such as EldenNet's subtitles is not a view the player opened | S10.4.c; D78; invariant 9 |
| D144 | The second example and Dear ImGui's core for mods | `examples/hello-panel` (C++, invented): its own Dear ImGui 1.93.0, headless, with `ImGuiBackendFlags_RendererHasVtxOffset`; its atlas built once (`ImFontAtlasBuildMain`) and registered as its texture; a checker texture; a Noto Sans Mono face for the shared atlas; a panel; a list a frame from its own thread, every ImDrawList appended with each command keeping its vertex offset, and a line laid out from the shared atlas's glyph table. `gu_imgui_core` is Dear ImGui's core alone at the pinned files, with no backend, linked only by the example mods (`tools/check_safety.py`). Noto Sans and Noto Sans Mono Regular (OFL) are fetched pinned by SHA-256 from the Noto project's own repository (`cmake/fonts.cmake`) into the build tree's `bin\fonts\` and `bin\hello-panel\`, each with its OFL.txt, and credited in `NOTICE`; the repository keeps no font | Fonts committed to the repository; the example drawing with GraceUI's atlas only | The example shows both producers D76 names, and the harness reads its drawing back | S10.4.c; D76, D85, D22 |
| D145 | World-state claims and a grant never miss each other | The input half keeps no copy of the providers' claims: `request_input` reads the session's combined word (written under the providers' lock) before its compare-and-swap, and again after it behind a full fence; a claim of danger or loading found then, or input or the render path off, ends the new holder at once and the answer is not_now with that reason. Every writer (a provider's `set_world_state` or disconnect, input or the render path turning off) fences and then reads the newest conditions (`Capture::settle`), ending any holder they forbid. The cursor's confinement is undone by the granting thread when the holder ended between its grant and the confinement | Copies of the flags written after the providers' lock is released (the code before); `apply_world` under the lock | The last provider to apply its copy won, not the last to report: danger could be lost, and a holder granted in combat (invariant 9), or stick and refuse every request; a check-then-grant on one side and a store-then-close on the other can miss each other unless both are fenced (the store-buffer pattern) | Review M1, L10; D129, D143; invariant 9 |
| D146 | The window filter follows the bound swap chain's window | The init thread's first guess (D120) skips console windows (`ConsoleWindowClass`, `PseudoConsoleWindow`, the process's own console): conhost names the console's client as the window's owning process. When the bound swap chain's window (its top-level window) is not the one filtered, GraceUI's own thread filters it, in place of the first; the window procedure's filter becomes one subclass per window given (at most four, EST), only the newest active, an earlier one passing every message on, and never removed; a holder ends when the filter moves (focus lost) | Filtering only the first window found; removing the earlier subclass | A console, splash or loader window found first left input off for the session, or the cursor clipped to the wrong rectangle and Dear ImGui's mouse mis-scaled; removing a subclass under another mod's is unsafe, and a pass-through one costs one comparison | Review M2; D120, D125 |
| D147 | GraceUI's own thread | The init thread stays for the session once the hooks are in, asleep on an auto-reset event any thread sets without waiting (every 50 ms if the event could not be made): it bakes the shared atlas (D148), moves the window filter (D146) and passes the log to `OutputDebugString` (the ring stays the log; a line is never sent by the thread that wrote it). The first request makes the event before the thread starts | A thread made for each job; the present thread doing them | Each is work a game thread must not wait on: a bake takes hundreds of milliseconds, and `OutputDebugString` waits for a listener (up to 10 s) or a debugger; one sleeping thread costs nothing | Review M2, M3, L2; D120; invariant 10 |
| D148 | The atlas's room, and its bake off the present thread | A face's estimated area: the glyphs its font's character map gives its ranges (the subtable stb_truetype reads, formats 0, 4, 6, 12 and 13; every code point of the ranges otherwise), each as a box of (2 × size + 2) by (size + 2) pixels, counted when the face is committed. The mods' faces share 8192 × 4096 estimated pixels and the mods other than EldenNet half of that (EST); a face past either answers `full`. Warm-up seals the faces and asks GraceUI's own thread, which makes Dear ImGui's context, bakes, and should Dear ImGui still run out of room bakes again on a fresh atlas with half the mods' faces, then none of theirs (a log line names the first face left out); then makes the atlas's texture and its upload buffer on the game's device, fills it, and frees the CPU copy (`ClearTexData`). The present thread draws nothing meanwhile, then records the one copy | Face counts alone (the code before); an Alpha8 atlas, a quarter of the memory; baking on the present thread within a smaller room | Dear ImGui's atlas stops at 8192 × 8192: four 128 px faces of a Latin font's whole BMP asserted ("Out of texture memory"), which turned the overlay off for every mod, and two took 544 ms on the present thread (the review's probe). The estimate is an upper bound for most glyphs, so a full room bakes into well under the limit. An Alpha8 atlas would need the DX12 backend's shader changed (an R8 texture samples as red), which D107 forbids. A CJK face at 24 px (about 27 million estimated pixels) fits only while the other mods use little: EldenNet registers only the ranges it needs; S4 measures the bake's time and the atlas's size in the game | Review M3; D117, D139, D80; S4 |
| D149 | Letting go of the game's device | When the bound device is removed (`GetDeviceRemovedReason`, each frame) or a swap chain on another device is bound, the render path turns off, and GraceUI releases everything it made on the old device (the DX12 backend's objects, its slots, heaps, fence, atlas and textures) and the device itself, at once for a removed device and otherwise once its last frame's fence has passed, never waiting; a bake running on GraceUI's thread is waited for frame by frame. The queues held for D116's ways are let go once the render path is off, and none is held after | Keeping the first device for the session (the code before); rebuilding on the new device | Direct3D 12 hands out one device per adapter while a reference lives: GraceUI's would hand a game recovering from a removal the removed device again, and keep a replaced one's memory. Rebuilding is left to a later version: the mods' textures' pixels are freed after their upload, so a rebuild would need them registered again (or 64 MiB kept for the session) | Review L3; D118, D119 |
| D150 | When the host or the render path is off, so is input | A host that gives up after MinHook enabled some hooks (it stops at the first failure) passes every call through those hooks untouched, and turns input off; the render path turning off turns input off too: every detour and the filter pass everything, the menu key included, and any holder ends (fault) | Disabling the hooks MinHook enabled; leaving the menu key masked | GraceUI never unhooks while the game runs (D107); with no overlay nothing can open a menu, and the game or player that binds F5 should have it | Review L1, L4; D120, D129 |
| D151 | The watchdog hears frames drawn | The render path tells the input half's watchdog only of a frame it drew, or one with nothing to draw; a frame skipped because its slot is still on the GPU, or because the present queue is changing, is not heard, so a holder whose view cannot be drawn for 2 s ends (no render) | Every frame the render path saw (the code before) | A holder could otherwise keep the controls with no view on screen while GraceUI's frames never complete | Review L5; D129 |
| D152 | An answer's size read once; a command stride bounded | A struct GraceUI fills has its size field read once, and what is written goes by that reading. A draw list's command stride is at most `GRACEUI_DRAW_COMMAND_SIZE_MAX` (256 bytes, EST), one more constant ABI 1.0 grows by (D140, D132's check lets it through) | Reading the size twice (the code before); no bound above | A mod's other thread could shrink the field between two reads and make the copy's length wrap; an unset or garbage stride sent the copy gigabytes past the mod's array, an access violation in GraceUI's code | Review L6; D131, D137, D140 |
| D153 | Smaller fixes from the review | D116's fourth way names a candidate by identity and takes its reference inside the candidate's own `ExecuteCommandLists` call; a queue not seen for 120 presents (EST) drops out of its count (L7). The render half's five targets' modules are pinned, as the input half's are (L8). A request made while the first is running waits 2 s at most (EST), then answers null with one log line (L9). After a present queue is replaced, nothing of GraceUI's goes to the new one until its last work on the old has passed (L11). A second copy's name too long for its notice keeps its end, after "...", from a character's start (L12). An ANSI window whose code page is UTF-8 has its bytes joined by UTF-8's rules (L13). The holder's panel is published and then asked for under the panels' lock, and a disconnecting mod's panels go before its holder is looked for; its slot is not given to another mod until its clean-up is done (L14) | Each finding's own alternatives, in the review | Each closes a narrow race or a misuse that could crash, hang or mislead; none changes what a well-behaved mod sees. Left as they are: a holder closed and another granted within the same microseconds may leave the first's saved clip for the second (Windows drops a clip on a foreground change); an HDR swap chain's colour space, which GraceUI cannot read back (S4 looks at it) | Review L7 to L14; D116, D119, D126, D135, D142 |
| D154 | The harness's frame costs on a loaded machine | A frame-cost check (frame cost, holder frame cost, notice allocations, panel frames) measures its frames in windows, each as long as its one window was, up to three (EST): its budget holds against the least p99 of the windows run, which stop at the first within it; every window's median and p99 are printed, and what a window must do besides (every frame drawn, at least so many panels or lists, no allocation, no event dropped) holds in every window run. The input costs measured with a holder are kept at their least p99 across the same windows. The figures stay wall time, less the runtime's own submission calls, as before | The present thread's CPU time (`QueryThreadCycleTime`, `GetThreadTimes`); the kernel's per-thread schedstat through Wine; a higher budget or percentile; a retry of the whole run | Interference only lengthens a frame's wall time, never shortens it, so the least of several windows is the tightest bound measured: a cost of GraceUI's own shows in every window, a busy spell of the machine's in one, and the budget and percentile are unchanged. Under Wine 9.0 no clock gives the thread's own time per frame: `QueryThreadCycleTime` fails, `GetThreadTimes` counts in 10 ms ticks, and the kernel's schedstat (read through Wine's Z: drive, 25 to 50 µs a read) updates a running thread's runtime a tick late and sees no time a virtual machine's host steals, which is where this machine's load shows (a probe of ours, 2026-11-16). On Windows, S4 can measure cycles (`QueryThreadCycleTime`) | Five runs on a loaded machine (load 2.2 to 3.5): two failed one check each, the panels' p99 1,033 µs and a functional one; BUILD.md |
| D155 | Hotkeys and a first `poll` in 0.1 | `register_hotkey` and `poll` come forward from 0.2 into ABI 1.0 (minor still 0, D74), for EldenNet's F11 view (its part 12, roadmap S10.4.d): `poll` is typed `GraceUiStatus (*)(GraceUiConsumer, GraceUiEvent*)`, a slot D131 reserved, answering at once one event or `GRACEUI_EVENT_NONE`, never waiting; `GraceUiEvent` starts with its size, then its kind, and in 0.1 carries one kind, `GRACEUI_EVENT_HOTKEY` (the hotkey's id and its presses since its last event), §6's kinds and their fields to follow after these. A hotkey is an action id (1 to 32 of a-z, 0-9 and '-', the mod's own), a display name, a default virtual key and modifiers; GraceUI maps the key to its DirectInput code once, at registration. Escape, the modifiers, the Windows keys and Alt+F4 are refused; a pad chord answers `not_yet` (0.1 takes keys alone); a default another hotkey or the menu key has taken registers unbound, with one log line, to be bound on the Hotkeys page (0.6); 8 a mod and 64 in the session (EST). With no holder, a fresh press of the key with exactly its modifiers fires it in the window procedure's filter, which counts it on the hotkey's own 64-bit word (state, key, modifiers, generation, presses) by a compare-and-swap, so no thread waits and a hotkey removed meanwhile counts nothing; the press, its repeats, characters and release never reach the game, and are hidden from DirectInput until released. A key bound with no modifier is never the game's while bound, as the menu key is not; while a holder has input hotkeys fire nothing (D77), a holder that takes the keyboard getting the key as any other. A press the game saw begin reaches it to its release. The modifiers are read (`GetKeyState`) only for a fresh press of a bound key with no holder: under Wine each read can cost 100 µs and more, which pushed the input-cost check's p99 past its 50 µs when every message read them. The cap is `hotkeys` (Amended by D164, 2026-11-20: an unbound hotkey answers `GRACEUI_UNBOUND`, with its id, and `GRACEUI_OK` means bound) (Amended by D204, 2026-11-22: a pad chord from a mod built for ABI 1.3 or later is taken; one built before still gets `not_yet`) | A hotkey-only slot of its own; leaving F11 to 0.2's protocol; EldenNet reading F11 itself (it would reach the game, invariant 8, or need a hook of EldenNet's on GraceUI's window procedure, invariant 16) | D77 already makes hotkeys the framework's and D73 names both slots; typing `poll` now with a size-first event that later kinds extend keeps D73's order and the append-only rule, and needs no new slot | S10.4.d; D73, D77, D131, D142 |
| D156 | A mod's headless Dear ImGui, the third example, and two examples at once | `graceui-sys` gains `graceui_imgui.hpp`, header-only C++ for a mod that compiles Dear ImGui's core (1.93.0) with no backend: `prepare(io)` sets the flags GraceUI's renderer needs (`ImGuiBackendFlags_RendererHasVtxOffset`, no ini or log file); `register_atlas(api, me, io)` bakes the mod's font atlas once and registers it as one of its textures in its registration window, telling Dear ImGui it is on the GPU so it never asks for an update (invariant 11); `DrawList::build(draw_data, width, height)` appends every ImDrawList of a frame into one GraceUI list in the panel's own pixels, each command keeping its vertex offset and its clip held within the panel, and answers false, building nothing, for a frame past ABI 1's bounds, which GraceUI would refuse; `view()` is the list `submit` takes. `hello-panel` now uses it. The third example, `examples/hello-debug-view` (C++, invented), is EldenNet's banner and F11 view in miniature: a banner under its own key with a duration of 0, its text replaced in place as the view opens and closes; F11 through `register_hotkey`, its presses taken through `poll`; a hidden panel anchored at the bottom left on its own headless Dear ImGui, given its first list before it is shown. The harness runs it with `hello-notice` in two processes, each loaded once before GraceUI and once after the hooks ("examples", "examples-reversed"): both connect, both notices are drawn in the order they connected and nothing else, F11 opens the view (drawn where its anchor puts it, the banner's text replaced, F11 kept from DirectInput and the window's messages) and closes it again | Each mod writing its own conversion, as `hello-panel` did; a compiled helper library; a C helper | The conversion is the same for every mod on Dear ImGui and is where a mod would get the bounds, the clip or the vertex offset wrong; header-only C++ adds no library to version and builds against the Dear ImGui the mod compiles (D76); EldenNet's part 12 uses it as the example does | S10.4.d; D76, D134, D144, D155 |
| D157 | The tokens file and its build step | `tokens/grace.toml` (schema 1) holds every token of §3.2 to §3.10 by role: each variant's colours, Grace the default and Grace Clear, which names Grace as its base and gives only what differs (its ground.well, ink.hi, ink.mute, ink.faint at ink.mute's value since it has no faint level, its 2 px edges and 3 px focus ring), each colour `#RRGGBB` or a mix (a token or a hex at an alpha, composited over whatever ground is under it, the alpha a byte in both views); the contrast rules themselves (the text tokens, the grounds, 4.5:1, the edges at 3:1, the named pairs, the tokens never used for text); fonts by role, the type scale with its minimums (D23), spacing, frame sizes, radii, and durations, each a fade, a transition, a hold or instant. `tools/tokens/build.py` (Python, standard library only) checks contrast in every variant, the type minimums (display faces at 20 px or more, serif at the prose minimum, capitals at the eyebrow minimum), the durations (0 to 2,000 ms, instant exactly 0, nothing that repeats) and every stylesheet and script under `web/src` for a looping animation, then writes `web/src/tokens.css` (custom properties, one block per variant under `data-theme`, and reduced motion under both `prefers-reduced-motion` and `[data-reduce-motion]`: every duration 0 but a fade, at most 60 ms) and `src/grace-tokens/include/grace/tokens.h` (a `Palette` and `Sizes` per variant as constexpr values, `over()` rounding as the tool does, the type scale, spacing, sizes, radii and durations with their reduced values). Both outputs are committed; `--check` fails CTest when either drifts, and CTest runs the tool's own tests (§3.3's table reproduced: the design rounds each ratio to 0.01 and then to 0.1, so a cell may sit 0.05 above the ratio rounded once; each check failing on tokens seeded to break it) and the header's. 0.1's notices draw from the header (`grace-host` links `grace-tokens`), the same values as before | Tokens as JSON; generating the outputs at build time only; a CSS-first source | TOML is what GraceUI's other files use, and Python's standard library reads it; committed outputs keep the web build free of Python and let the drift check say what changed; the contrast rules beside the colours make a new token checked as soon as it is added | S11.2.e; §3.3, §3.15, D108, invariants 13 and 14 |
| D158 | grace-serve's pure core | `grace_serve_core` (`src/grace-serve`, pure: no sockets, clocks, threads, or allocation in the parser and decoder): a request-head parser fed incrementally into its own 8 KiB buffer, with a request target of at most 1,024 bytes (414), at most 64 fields and a Cookie field of at most 4 KiB (431), and a Content-Length of at most 32 MiB by default, each route's own cap below it (413); a NUL, a bare CR, a bare LF or an empty first line is refused as it arrives (400), so a client that never ends a head does not wait out its deadline; then the syntax, then Host (421), then the method (405), the framing (501, 400, 413) and Expect (417); a POST needs exactly one Content-Length and a GET none or 0; Host, Content-Length, Cookie, Origin, Content-Type, Upgrade and the Sec-WebSocket-* and Sec-Fetch-* fields twice is 400; a query string is reported for a 404. One writer puts item 10's headers on every response head (at most 1 KiB, the redirect's included); the redirect is 307 with `no-store`, its Location built from the listed name (item 23); the 101 carries `Connection: Upgrade` and no Content-Length, a 426 `Connection: Upgrade, close`. Host and Origin match byte for byte, the loopback origin always `http://127.0.0.1:PORT` (item 23's one origin); Origin, Fetch Metadata and cookie refusals are 403; a page load is a GET whose Sec-Fetch-Mode is navigate (a frame's navigation is not top-level) or one with no Fetch Metadata. The session cookie and a ticket are 32 random bytes (the I/O part's) as 43 characters of unpadded base64url, compared in constant time, every `en_session` in the field tried. Sec-WebSocket-Accept from our own SHA-1 and Base64. WebSocket frames per RFC 6455, strict: masked, no RSV bit, a known opcode, control frames whole and at most 125 bytes, minimal lengths, the 64-bit length's high bit clear, text UTF-8 across fragments, close codes valid (1012 to 1014 taken as registered); a message's 16 KiB cap is checked from each frame's header before its payload is read; failures close with 1002, 1007 or 1009 | Deciding everything only once the head is whole; waiting out the deadline on a bare LF; one buffer a frame; a library's parser (G30) | Pure, fixed-size, allocation-free and fuzzable (§7.6), so the risk G30 accepted, bugs in our own parsing, is met by the review's plan (SRV §5): every refusal tested with its status, a chunking differential, the RFC vectors, and two libFuzzer targets that feed each input whole, byte by byte and in random splits and trap unless the three agree (384,505 and 395,309 runs in 60 s, none failing) | S11.2.g; §7.2 items 3 to 7 and 19 to 23, §6.8, G30, SRV §5 |
| D159 | The protocol's schema and grace-protocol | One schema, `src/grace-protocol/protocol.toml` (TOML, as the tokens): the protocol's version, §6.8's limits, the enums (error codes, bye reasons, setting types and effects), the shared types (a space's settings schema, `space_info`, a choice, a setting of §6.9, the `settings` topic's body), every kind of §6.3 with who sends it and whether it carries its space, and the framework's own commands (`settings.set`, `settings.reset` as a scope with a target, `menu.close`, `dashboard.disconnect`); a space's own topics and commands join it as their pages are built (EldenNet's with its pages, S11.4), and until then a body is a JSON object its space checks. Field types: string with a byte bound, id (1 to 64 of a-z, 0-9, '.', '_', '-', '/'), u32, u64 (at most 2^53 − 1, so the web app's numbers hold it exactly), f64 (finite), bool, json and object with a byte bound, enum, type, and list with an item bound. `tools/protocol/generate.py` checks the schema (every type it names exists, every string and list bounded, no field twice, no envelope field) and writes the C++ types with checked decoders and text encoders and the web app's TypeScript types (`web/src/protocol.ts`), all committed, `--check` failing CTest on drift and `tsc --strict` checking the TypeScript where it is installed. `grace-protocol` (pure; nlohmann/json 3.12.0, header-only, MIT, pinned by SHA-256, its implicit conversions off) parses a frame by its direction's limit (16 KiB from a page, 256 KiB to one), UTF-8, a nesting depth of 32 (EST, checked before parsing), JSON, an object, `v` (another major answers `version`), a known `t`, a kind that travels that way, and every field (unknown fields ignored, §6.4); a result is `ok: true` or names an error, not both. A refusal names the field and what was wrong, never the value; nothing throws. A frame-bounded body is moved out of the parsed frame, and frames are written straight to text, so the largest payloads are never copied: on this machine (GCC, -O2) a 250 KiB state frame then costs nlohmann's own parse, 3.3 ms, and its dump, 0.7 ms, against §8's 0.5 ms each (a risk in §11). Tests: golden frames (15 taken, each re-encoded byte for byte; 22 refused with their codes), a round trip of every kind, the commands, the refusals a text file cannot hold; a libFuzzer target that also checks a taken frame survives its own encoding (356,440 runs in 60 s, clean) | JSON Schema or a TypeScript-first source; C++ types as the source; our own JSON parser now | One schema file in the repository's own format, read by Python's standard library; nlohmann/json is what §2.2 names, MIT and long fuzzed upstream; whether its speed holds is a measurement for S4 on the player's hardware, with a faster parser or a body passed through unparsed as the ways out | S11.2.f; §6, D55, D108 |
| D160 | The page descriptions' format, GraceUI's own space and the demo space | A space's page data are protocol types (`protocol.toml`: `space_pages`, `category`, `page`, `page_tab`, `page_section`, `page_block`, `page_action`, `page_help`, `topic_info`, `settings_description`), so both views and the host read them through the same generated, checked types. A page holds its id, category, name, one-line description, help (its purpose and three to five steps), Related links (a page of its space, or of a built-in space as `<space>/<page>`), an increment or a tag (`wip`, `later`), up to four header actions (each a command of the space's, a destructive one with its confirmation) and its tabs or its sections; a section its blocks and an Advanced disclosure; a block is a settings block (the keys homed on this page), a shortcut block (keys homed elsewhere) or a component (§2.5's vocabulary, the enum `component`) bound to a declared topic of the shape it draws, with props. A space declares its topics (state, or stream with its ring) and its commands. Its source is one TOML file, `src/grace-pages/spaces/<id>/space.toml` for the built-in spaces; `tools/pages/build.py` checks it against the schema and by the page lint (unique ids, the rail, tabs or sections, help, links, every setting on exactly one settings block on its home page, shortcuts elsewhere, defaults of their type and within their bounds, a secret's default empty, availability as `live`, `later`, `inert`, `check:<id>`, `version:<x.y>` or `requires:<key>`, a later setting locked, a tri-state `on`, `off` or `auto`, components and topics, actions, fixtures) and writes `fixtures/<id>/pages.json` and `settings.json` and `src/grace-pages/builtin.cpp`, all committed, drift failing CTest; `--lint FILE` checks another project's spaces, EldenNet's in its CI. The settings schema's hash is the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the description's canonical JSON. GraceUI's own space (`graceui`): one category, its six pages of D79 (Menu and Browser view in 0.2, the rest in 0.6), and its eight settings of D84 and D115 (`ui.scale` 0.75 to 1.5, `ui.menu.theme`, `ui.menu.reduce_motion`, `ui.menu.plain_text`, `hotkeys.menu`, `dashboard.enabled`, `dashboard.port`, `dashboard.lan` locked until 0.6). The demo space (`demo`, D82): a lantern keeper's invented pages, every setting type, tabs, an Advanced disclosure, state and stream topics with fixtures, a locked setting, one waiting on a check, a later page and a destructive action | Page data in JSON by hand; a page format of its own beside the protocol; the lint in C++ | One set of types for pages and frames, generated for both views; TOML is the repository's format for hand-written data; the lint in Python runs where EldenNet's pages are written, with no build | S11.2.h; §2.5, §4.2.4, §4.7, §6.9, D79, D82, D115, invariant 12 |
| D161 | grace-serve, the server | `grace_serve` (`src/grace-serve/io/`, linking the pure core, which stays pure: it compiles nothing of io/ and includes none of its headers). **Threads:** one non-blocking I/O thread over WSAPoll or poll(2), woken by an eventfd or, on Windows, a UDP socket on 127.0.0.1 connected to itself; one worker that reads and checks the web files at start; each catches every exception at its top; start and stop are ordinary calls, never from DllMain, and stop closes every socket and joins both. **Listeners** on 127.0.0.1 and `::1` where the system has it, bound by name, `SO_EXCLUSIVEADDRUSE` on Windows and never `SO_REUSEADDR`, every socket non-inheritable; a taken port fails closed with one line naming both causes; `[::1]` held by another program leaves 127.0.0.1 alone, with one log line. **Slots:** a fixed pool of 24: 16 plain HTTP connections (a 17th waits in the backlog), 2 WebSockets (a third supersedes the oldest with `bye superseded`), a complete head within 5 s of accept, `Connection: close`, a send queue of 512 KiB (EST). **`graceui.toml`** read as a strict TOML subset (files.h; D165 writes it): only `[files]` entries under `web/` are served, at `/<name>`, `web/index.html` at `/` too; names of A-Z a-z 0-9 and `-._~/`, no dot-led segment, nothing under `api/`; twelve known extensions; 16 MiB a file and 64 MiB in all (EST); any failure withholds the whole app and page loads get a built-in 503 notice page. **Cookie:** on 200 and 404 page loads with Host 127.0.0.1 from a peer of exactly 127.0.0.1 or `::1`, never on redirects, refusals or the notice page. **Ticket:** `POST /api/ticket` answers `{"ticket": …}` (43 characters); a JSON body of at most 256 bytes, not read; one use, 10 s (EST), at most 8 held, void on renewal. **Socket:** `GET /api/ws`, hello with the ticket within 2 s of the 101. **Spaces** (`space.h`): a host adds a `SpaceDescription` (from a space's page data and settings description) and gets a `SpacePort`, polled for `watched`, `unwatched` and `command` events, with `publish` (State, Rows) and `answer` (Result), for graceui.dll, grace-dev and EldenNet's launcher (G29). **Limits:** states coalesced to 4 a second per view, the newest; the server keeps each watched stream's ring for later watchers (`since` inclusive) and nothing unwatched; 20 messages a second counted before parsing (subs, pings and hello included, so a page paces a burst of subscriptions); 32 subscriptions; 32 commands queued per space, the newest refused `rate_limited`; 3 bad messages or 2 missed pings close with no bye; a sub refusal is an `error` frame whose detail names the sub's id (the protocol's `error` has no id field yet). grace-serve's frame fuzzer is `grace_ws_frame_fuzz`. **Not yet:** §7.6's hardening flags (CFG, stack cookies, the hardened library) come with graceui.dll's link settings; paired devices' sensitive commands with §7.2 item 16 | A thread per connection; a fallback port; files read per request | §7.6's two threads, fixed and failing closed; every rule on the wire is the core's (D158) | S11.2.i; §6.3 to §6.8, §7.2 items 1 to 15, 17 and 19 to 24; D111, D112, D158 |
| D162 | The in-game views, grace-view and grace-draw | `grace-view` (pure) loads a space's page data, settings description and values into a `PageView`: the frame (the rail as an accordion of the open page's category, the top bar with search and the closing key, the page clipped between them and scrolled to keep focus in view, the key hints for the focused control) and one page (its header and actions, tabs, section cards with their Advanced disclosure, settings rows of 48 px with the control in its 280 px column, shortcut rows, the Related card), laid out with the tokens at the viewport's size and `ui.scale` into a display list whose items name their colours by role, so a theme is the palette the host draws with and needs no new layout. Controls: a switch, a slider with its value, a segmented choice or tri-state, a key cap, a field for text and secrets; a value changed from its default shows the lozenge; the help line opens on focus and ends with the default; a locked setting is dimmed with its badge, one waiting on a check badged; a refused command shows its reason in `rot` under its control. Navigation (§5.3, §5.4, §5.7): three scopes (rail, top bar, page), ↑↓, ← → changing a value (Shift by ten steps) or, elsewhere, ← back to the rail, Tab and Shift+Tab across scopes, Enter, Esc to the rail and then closing, Q and E for tabs, Page Up and Page Down in rail order across categories, R to reset; a destructive action asks for a second press; opening a page focuses its first control; every change is a `settings.set` or `settings.reset` command, ready to send. Search over the settings' labels, synonyms and keys. Text from the atlas's glyph tables (each role drawn from its face's bake, scaled, with its tracking added to every advance; an ellipsis ends a run cut short). Not yet in game, until GraceUI's own text field and key capture (D57, v0.6): editing a text, secret or key setting, and the components, which show "Shown in the browser view.". `grace-draw` (pure) tessellates the display list into ABI 1's draw-list layout, so GraceUI's menu passes the same checker as a mod's panel: rounded fills, strokes, discs and the lozenge with a one-pixel fringe fading to nothing, hairlines on whole device pixels, a quad a glyph, solid shapes from the atlas's white pixel so a page is one texture, a new command only for a clip or texture change or past 65,536 vertices. A second frame allocates nothing; the layout allocates as it builds, so it runs off the present thread, when the page or its values change, and the present thread only copies the newest list (S11.2.b) | ImGui's own widgets; a layout library; laying out in the present thread every frame | `grace-view` takes no ImGui (§2.2), so its layout and navigation are tested on Linux, as the structure dumps of §9.3 need; one checker for every list keeps GraceUI's own drawing within the bounds it holds mods to; the menu at 1920 × 1080 is 66 items, 2,720 vertices and 4 commands | S11.2.l; §3.16, §4.2, §4.7, §5.3, §5.4, §5.7, §9.3, D76, invariant 10 |
| D163 | A tri-state that inherits | Asked for by EldenNet's session on 2026-11-20, for its allowances and per-reaction switches (its PAGES, Named NPCs and NPC types, and its DESIGN §7.6's chain: named slot, type, live kind, base kind, shipped default). A tri-state setting names its third value, `third`: `auto` (the default, the space deciding at the moment) or `inherit` (taken from a chain the space resolves); its values are `on`, `off` and its third, its default one of them (the page lint). For an inherit tri-state on `inherit`, the settings topic's body carries `inherited`: each such key, whether it resolves on or off, its source in words and, optionally, the page that sets it. Both views show it faint beside the row ("On, from …"), the browser with the source as a link; ← → cycle the three values in game and X or R resets the row, as for any tri-state. The demo space's Wind shield is one | A choice of three for the inherited case; the resolved value as a topic of its own | The third value is the only difference, so one type with a named third keeps one control and one rule; the resolved value belongs with the values it explains, in the topic every settings page already watches | S11.2.h, S11.4.a; §6.9, D160 |
| D164 | From EldenNet's part 12: an unbound hotkey answers so, and graceui-sys says when GraceUI is absent | EldenNet's part 12, built against f51bcc7 and run end to end under Wine (2026-11-20), asked for these. (1) `register_hotkey` answers a status of its own, `GRACEUI_UNBOUND` (11), with the hotkey's id, for a hotkey registered but unbound (its default taken by another hotkey or the menu key, or none), and `GRACEUI_OK` only for one that is bound; the mod can then keep its own fallback (EldenNet's launcher keeps its Pause key when GraceUI's Pause is unbound). ABI 1 is a preview (D131), so the change is taken in place; the constant is new, the table and its layout unchanged. (2) `graceui-sys`'s `GraceUiSysFound` gains `installed`, at the last look 1 if a module named `graceui.dll` was loaded or `GraceUI\graceui.dll` was in the game's folder, and the flag `GRACEUI_SYS_STOP_IF_ABSENT` ends the looking at the first look that finds neither and nothing answering, so the rule "not installed, stop at once" lives in the helper, not in each mod; the harness's "absent" scenario shows it (one look, against a full wait without the flag). (3) A mod's Dear ImGui core brings imports of its own: the runtime's thread-safe statics (an SRW lock and a condition variable) and `ReadConsoleW` through `sscanf`; a mod that checks its imports builds the core with `/Zc:threadSafeInit-`, which removes the first four (the guide says so, G18). (4) EldenNet keeps `graceui-sys`'s headers, `graceui_test.h` among them, as a vendored copy pinned by SHA-256 rather than fetched, so no account name enters its files; the test structs keep their size fields, so a later layout breaks nothing silently (Amends D155: `register_hotkey` answers unbound so; and D136) | An out flag on `register_hotkey` (the slot's type is fixed); a query slot; leaving the rule to each mod | A distinct status is what every other slot does to say what happened; the helper is where finding GraceUI is decided | S10.4.d, S11.2; D131, D136, D155 |
| D165 | The version file's writer | `tools/release/manifest.py` writes `graceui.toml` (D52) for a GraceUI folder, from CMakeLists.txt's version, `graceui.h`'s ABI minor and `protocol.toml`'s version and caps (none yet: `caps = []` until the protocol declares some), listing every file but itself and `user\` (D84), sorted, within the strict subset `grace-serve` reads (§2.7); a name that would need an escape, or that is not segments of A-Z a-z 0-9 and `-._~`, fails the write, as a link does. `check` names each missing, extra or changed file, for EldenNet's release job and its tests. Every Windows build lays out `GraceUI\` beside `bin\` (the DLL, `LICENSE.txt`, `NOTICE.txt` and the file), so a consumer's tests install GraceUI's own folder instead of writing a version file of their own (EldenNet's part 12 asked for this, 2026-11-20); a test build's folder is never a release | Each consumer writing the file; the file written by CMake alone | One writer, beside the reader's rules, keeps the format in one place; Python, as the repository's other tools | S11.2; D52, D83, D84 |
| D166 | The browser view's web shell | `web/`: Preact 11.2.0, @preact/signals 2.11.4 (with signals-core 1.14.5), TypeScript 7.0.2 and Vite 7.3.8, all MIT but TypeScript (Apache-2.0); exact pins, a committed lockfile, installs from it with scripts off (`.npmrc`), `npm audit` clean, 19 packages in all; `tools/web/licences.py` checks the installed tree against a reviewed allowlist and for AI clauses, and writes the bundle's `licences.txt` from the packages actually bundled (NOTICE lists the three). Vite 7, not 8: Vite 8 brings lightningcss, MPL-2.0, off the allowlist. No test-runner package: logic tests on `node:test` with Node's type stripping, DOM tests in the Chromium already on the machine, behind a fake host with the real CSP, and a bundle scan (no remote URL, no `eval`). The frame and every page part are drawn only from page descriptions (invariant 12): the rail as an accordion with GraceUI's space at its foot, collapsed under 1100 px with flyouts and a space switcher from two content spaces (D79); the top bar with search, the status lamp only when a space publishes a `status` topic (`{word, tone, reason, page}`) and the theme switch; the header, help dialog, tabs, sections, Advanced, every control, shortcuts, the components, Related, empty states. **One `Transport`**: fixtures for the tests and the dev page, and the WebSocket: `POST /api/ticket`, then `/api/ws` with hello first, on its own origin only (invariant 6). Each space's page data come from the framework's topic `pages`, which grace-serve answers itself from what the space registered (D169), and its values from `settings`. An edit shows "Saving…" until the space answers; a refusal returns focus to its row with the reason (§5.7). Values are formatted and keys pressed as grace-view does them, checked against goldens grace-view's own tests write (`rows-order.txt`, `formats.txt`, §9.3). Fonts: the system's until the faces ship as WOFF2. **Follow-ups, settled here by the design**, for both views together: a destructive action sends `confirm: true` after its second press, and GraceUI refuses a destructive command without it `confirm_required` (§7.3); a page joins the rail in the increment it ships in, its later pages absent (§4.2.2); a secret's status reads Set or Missing, and the badges use §4.7's words (§4.2.4); a category's icon is a protocol field in a later minor (the collapsed rail draws the category's initial until then); the slider's typed entry (§3.8) | Vite 8 (an MPL dependency); a test-runner package; Preact's Vite preset; key order copied by hand from C++ | The smallest audited tree; parity checked against the game view's own code | S11.2.k; §2.5, §4.2, §4.3, §5.9, §6, §7.2 to §7.5, §9.3; D79, D157, D160, D162, D163, D169 |
| D167 | The in-game menu, for S4 (S11.2.b) | GraceUI's menu is the input holder of kind menu that GraceUI itself asks for (owner 0; a test's holder of that kind is not it). A fresh press of the menu key (F5) with no holder asks for input as any holder does; granted, the menu opens on the page and row it was left on in the session (first, GraceUI › Menu, focus on its first control); not now, one of GraceUI's own notices says why ("Not now: you are in danger.", §5.1), and before the atlas holds the menu's faces, or after a fault turned the menu off, it says so. **Three threads, none waiting**: the window procedure turns the menu's keys (§5.3: arrows, Tab, Enter and Space, Esc, Q and E, Page Up and Page Down, R; search waits for GraceUI's text field, §5.6) into grace-view's inputs on a ring of its own, the present thread does the same for the pad's new presses (§5.4: d-pad and left stick, A, B, LB and RB, LT and RT, X; a press held at the grant does nothing until released), and GraceUI's own thread, woken by either, runs `grace-view`'s page view and GraceUI's settings store, lays the page out at the effective scale `(H / 1080) × ui.scale` (§3.13) and tessellates it with `grace-draw` in Grace or Grace Clear into a draw list's triple buffer, as a mod's thread submits a panel's; the present thread lends the newest list for the holder now to Dear ImGui's renderer in GraceUI's own layer, above the mods' hud and panel layers and below their modal layer and the notices (D76), and allocates nothing (invariant 10). A list laid out for an earlier holder is never drawn. **One entry point** (invariant 3): `grace-pages`' `SettingsStore`, pure and unit-tested, holds a space's values, checks every value against its description (type, range, step, choices, locked or not live) and answers `settings.set` and `settings.reset` (a page or all only when confirmed) with the protocol's codes and words that never quote a value, a secret write-only (invariant 5); a refusal puts focus back on the row with its reason (§5.7). `ui.scale` and `ui.menu.theme` take effect at once; the other settings are kept for their users. **Closing** is grace-input's one close routine whoever ends it (Esc from the rail, F5, danger, loading, focus loss, no render, a fault, the player's exit); the menu's thread, at its next wake, cancels a confirmation waiting for its second press and keeps the page and row; changes stay. A fault in the menu turns it off for the session and ends its holder (fault). **Faces**: the atlas bakes one face for each of the eleven type roles at its size times the effective scale at warm-up, from GraceUI's fonts folder: Cinzel Regular and Bold (Cinzel publishes no static 500 or 600; Regular serves 500 and Bold 600), EB Garamond Regular, Medium and SemiBold, Noto Sans Regular and SemiBold, Noto Sans Mono Regular, each fetched pinned from its publisher with its OFL (NOTICE); Noto Sans and Cinzel take the four arrows (U+2190 to U+2193) merged from Noto Sans Mono; a missing file falls back to Noto Sans Regular with one log line; a bake that runs out of room drops the mods' faces first, then the menu's, so the notices keep their face. A change of `ui.scale` scales the nearest bake until the next session. Measured under Wine on lavapipe at 1920 × 1080 (the harness's "menu" scenario): the atlas 1024 × 2048; the page 2752 vertices, 6612 indices, 4 commands, laid out and tessellated in about 0.06 to 0.3 ms on GraceUI's thread; the present thread's own work, median about 0.15 ms at 1920 × 1080 (its p99 there is the software renderer's, which shares the machine's cores) and p99 under 1 ms at 640 × 360, with nothing allocated. **Not yet**: GraceUI's settings are kept for the session only (`user\`'s settings file, D84, comes with the server's settings); the mouse moves Dear ImGui's cursor but does not yet hover or click; the 200 ms fade (§5.1) and pad repeat are later; the menu key is F5 until `hotkeys.menu` is read (Amended by D182, 2026-11-20: the mouse hovers, clicks, drags a slider and scrolls with the wheel) | A menu laid out on the present thread (it allocates); the menu as a panel of a built-in consumer (a panel's anchors and checks are a mod's, and the menu is GraceUI's own layer); variable fonts instanced at build time (a converter in the build) | The present thread stays as the mods' lists keep it; the menu answers its settings where the browser's server will (one store); static faces as §3.16 says, with the nearest weights | S11.2.b; D76, D77, D79, D84, D115, D139, D162 |
| D168 | grace-dev, the fake host | `tools/grace-dev/` (a program and its own library, `grace_dev_core`, linked by it and its tests alone; built on Linux and in the Windows test builds) serves spaces on the real grace-serve: the built-in ones by id (`--space demo`, `--space graceui`, their fixtures compiled in) and an author's folder (`--space-dir`, pages.json, settings.json and an optional topics.json and scenario.json), each checked by the protocol's decoders, the settings store and the page view before it is served, a failure naming the file and the field, never a path. Each space keeps a `SettingsStore` and publishes its `settings` topic (with what an inherit tri-state resolves to, D163); fixture states (rev rising with each change) and streams (from the oldest row held) go out when watched, and a topic with no fixture publishes nothing; the `pages` topic is the server's own (D169). Every command is answered once: settings by the store, its words in `body.detail` (in `detail` since D172); `dashboard.disconnect` renews the session; `menu.close` is `not_now`; a space's own commands follow the scenario's reactions or are answered ok. **Scripted time**: a scenario is JSON (format 1) of timed steps (`state`, `rows`, `clear`) and reactions to commands, checked whole before it plays (a bad step named by file and line), played at `--speed`, with `--loop`; the demo's is `fixtures/demo/scenario.json`, invented. Web files from a folder's `graceui.toml`, a plain folder from memory (`web/dist` during development), or a placeholder page. Loopback only, on `dashboard.port`'s default and bounds; it prints no value, cookie, ticket or path; Ctrl+C and SIGTERM stop it cleanly. **`--dump SPACE PAGE --size WxH [--scale] [--theme]`** prints a page's in-game structure dump and its draw list's counts in the layout tests' face, so authors see the in-game structure without Windows (the C mock and a consumer over the ABI come later). check_safety's check 9 holds its code to checks 1 to 4. grace-view's structure dump rounds half to even itself, so it reads the same under every C runtime. Checked on 2026-11-20 with the built web app in Chromium: the demo's scenario played on the pages, nothing requested outside the origin, no error | A library in `src/`; fixtures read from a checkout at run time; JSON Lines scenarios; a real face in `--dump` (a rasteriser only the Windows builds fetch); an empty rows batch for `clear` (the protocol refuses one, so a gap in the numbering stands for it) | A developer's tool that needs no checkout beside it, the server's rules untouched, one validating entry point per space | S11.2.j; §9.2; D85, D161, D163, D167, D169 |
| D169 | The ABI's protocol slots, and the browser view's server in graceui.dll | Agreed with EldenNet's session on 2026-11-20. **One JSON path for both views** (§6.2): the same frames as the WebSocket cross the ABI. `register_pages(consumer, GraceUiSpaceDesc*)` takes the mod's one space (its views, `GRACEUI_VIEW_GAME` and `GRACEUI_VIEW_BROWSER`; its version and increment, named in welcome; its page data and settings description as JSON, copied, 1 MiB each at most, EST): refused if they fail the protocol's decoders, if pages and settings disagree or a default fails (grace-view's and the settings store's own checks), if its `space` is not the mod's id or it names no view; `duplicate_id` for a second space of a mod or an id another holds; `full` past 16 spaces (EST); `inactive` in the notice-only mode. `publish` takes a state or rows frame of the mod's own space, of a topic it declares and of that topic's shape, at most `GRACEUI_FRAME_TO_PAGE_MAX` (256 KiB): dropped if no view watches it. `poll` keeps hotkeys first, then gives the space's events into the event's new fields (`reply`, `frame`, `frame_capacity`, `frame_length`, appended, so a 0.1 mod's 16-byte event sees hotkeys alone): `GRACEUI_EVENT_WATCHED` and `_UNWATCHED` with the topic's sub or unsub frame (watched by any view, then by none, so a space builds nothing unwatched, §2.4), `_COMMAND` with the cmd frame and a reply handle; a buffer below the frame answers `full` with the size needed, and the event waits (`GRACEUI_FRAME_FROM_PAGE_MAX`, 16 KiB, always suffices). `answer(consumer, reply, frame)` takes the result frame, sets its space and id, and routes it to the one view that asked, once; a command unanswered for `GRACEUI_ANSWER_DEADLINE_MS` (10 s, EST) is answered `internal` with one log line naming the space (EldenNet's slow commands answer at once and publish progress). Any of the four from any thread of the mod's, during warm-up too, never from DllMain; none waits for a view. `GRACEUI_CAP_PAGES` names them. **grace-spaces** (`src/grace-spaces/`, no Win32, tested on Linux against the real server and under Wine): the registry of mods' spaces (thread-safe, one lock, never held across a view) and `OwnSpace`, GraceUI's own space, whose one settings store the in-game menu and the browser's views share: the settings topic goes out after every change, whichever view made it, and the menu follows a change the browser made at its next wake. GraceUI's own space answers `settings.set` and `settings.reset` through the store, `dashboard.disconnect` by renewing the server's session, and `menu.close` with `not_now` in the browser. The store refuses a later setting `locked` ("Only in a later version."), a locked one `locked`, and one a check holds `not_now` (amends D167's store). **The server in graceui.dll**: GraceUI's own thread starts grace-serve (D161) while a mod's space asks for the browser view, `dashboard.enabled` is on and the session is not in the notice-only mode, on `dashboard.port` (8642), serving the web files of GraceUI's folder as its `graceui.toml` lists them (D165), and stops it when none asks or the player turns it off: GraceUI's own space alone opens no socket (a reading of §7.2 item 1: the player's "on" is `dashboard.enabled`, which is on by default, so a space's asking is what starts it). A port that fails is not tried again until the next launch, with one notice. While it runs, GraceUI's own thread wakes about once a second for the deadlines. Test builds lay out a stand-in `web/index.html` in the GraceUI folder until the web app's build exists. **Not yet**: the in-game menu shows GraceUI's own space alone; a mod's space with `GRACEUI_VIEW_GAME`, its rail first and GraceUI's at its foot with a switcher from two (D79), comes next, through the same events | A C struct per message kind; a callback per event; a queue of GraceUI's own between the server and the mod (the server's port already queues per space) | One code path for the space, as the server's; nothing allocated or parsed twice; the frames are the protocol's own, checked by the same decoders | S11.2.b; §6.2, §7.2 item 1; D73, D75, D79, D81, D161, D165, D167 |
| D170 | graceui.dll imports no Direct3D DLL; its imports reviewed | From EldenNet's part 12 (2026-11-20): under Wine 9, loading graceui.dll before the game's first window can hang inside the loader's LoadLibraryW of dxgi.dll while other threads make USER calls (3 runs of 4 from a fake game's main thread with another thread polling EnumWindows; never alone), most likely a lock-order inversion between Wine's loader lock and its display driver's start-up. So graceui.dll imports none of dxgi.dll, d3d12.dll and d3dcompiler_47.dll: CreateDXGIFactory1, D3D12CreateDevice and D3DCompile are bound on the init thread, once the game's window exists and before the hook targets are read, from the modules the game has loaded or else from System32 alone (`LOAD_LIBRARY_SEARCH_SYSTEM32`, never the game's folder); Dear ImGui's DX12 backend's own calls to CreateDXGIFactory1 and D3DCompile reach stand-ins of GraceUI's that call the bound functions (the backend unchanged, D28), so the present thread loads nothing; if one cannot be bound, the host gives up with one log line. `tools/check_safety.py --exports` checks graceui.dll's imports against a reviewed list (invariant 16): kernel32, user32, gdi32, imm32, ws2_32, bcrypt, dwmapi, and MinGW's msvcrt. And graceui-sys's by-name find now sets `installed` (D164's rule; the branch returned before it) | Delay-loading the three (/DELAYLOAD has no counterpart in MinGW's GNU ld); patching the backend (D28); leaving it to mods to load GraceUI late | A mod may load GraceUI early under Proton, the platform that hangs; binding late costs nothing, and System32 alone is safer than the default search order | S11.2.b; D28, D71, D102, D164; invariant 16 |
| D171 | The in-game menu shows mods' spaces | The owner's go-ahead (2026-11-20: "go ahead with the menu showing a mod's pages"), on D79's rail and D169's slots. **Which, and in what order:** every space registered for the game's view (`GRACEUI_VIEW_GAME`), in the order the mods registered them, then GraceUI's own, last; one that goes while the menu shows it gives way to GraceUI's own. The session's first open is on the first mod's space, its first page; later opens are where the menu was left. **The rail** (D79): a mod's space has its wordmark and categories, and GraceUI's categories as links at its foot, under a rule; from two mods' spaces a switcher heads it ("Demo space, 1 of 2", ← and → open the previous and the next, focus staying on it); GraceUI's own space, opened from there, has the wordmark of the mod's space last open and its categories at its head, as links back. A link opens its space's page with focus on that page's entry on the rail, as opening a category does. **One path for both views** (invariant 3, §6.2): while the menu is open on a mod's space it watches that space's `settings` topic as a view does (the mod hears WATCHED when the first of the menu and the browser's views watches a topic and UNWATCHED when neither does, the menu's close included) and takes its newest state's `values` and `inherited`; its commands reach the mod as a view's do, with a reply handle and D169's 10 s deadline, and a refusal shows its words under the row, focus back on it, the row showing the mod's last published value again (or its default). Component blocks stay "Shown in the browser view." in game (G16). **Found on the way:** the input module's record of the last close lost the closed holder's phase, so a close by the menu key or the player's exit skipped the host's close bookkeeping (its log line, the cursor's restore); it now keeps it, and a close of the menu wakes GraceUI's thread, whose part of the close routine stops the menu's watches. | Every space in one long rail (crowded past two mods, and GraceUI's own lost among them); a menu of its own per mod (a second menu key, and each mod's settings out of reach of GraceUI's); the menu reading a mod's settings from its registration alone (stale the moment the mod changes one) | One menu key and one rail for every mod, GraceUI's own one step away; the mod's own state and its own validation for every change, as in the browser (invariant 3); nothing is built for the menu that the menu doesn't show | S11.2.b; D79, D161, D166, D169; invariants 3, 7, 17; tested by grace-view's rail cases, the registry's in-game cases and the harness's `menu-space` scenario |
| D172 | Protocol 1.1: a refusal's words, and a cleared stream | The owner's go-ahead (2026-11-20: "good ideas, do them"), on two of the questions put to them that day. **`result.detail`**, optional, a string of at most 256 bytes: the refusal in words a player reads, shown under the row in both views in place of the error code's words (§6.7), and by the in-game menu for mods' spaces (D171); GraceUI's own space, grace-dev and the registry use it, and `body.detail` is no longer written. **A cleared stream**: a `rows` batch whose `from_seq` is past a view's next row starts the stream over there (the server's replay ring already did, D161), so an empty batch past a gap clears the stream in every view at once; the server takes an empty batch and forwards it only to a view it clears, the web shell keeps each stream's next row and never shows a held row twice, and grace-dev publishes one when its scenario clears a stream. Minor 1 adds an optional field and gives a meaning to a batch the server refused before, so a 1.0 page still works (it misses the words and keeps cleared rows until the next batch); a page's `minor` (D160) is still the minor its components need | Words in `body.detail` (a convention a mod could miss, and body is the command's own); a `clear` message kind (a new kind for what the numbering already says); the server clearing on a command's success (it knows no command's meaning) | One field the schema checks, the same in every space; a stream's numbering stays its one source of truth | S11.2.b; §6.3, §6.4, §6.7; D160, D161, D168, D169, D171 |
| D173 | D166's follow-ups, in both views | Settled by D166 for both views together and done on 2026-11-20, before 0.2.0. **Destructive commands carry their confirmation** (§7.3): a destructive action's command (its tone `destructive`) is sent with `"confirm": true` after its second press, by grace-view and the web shell alike; the server refuses one without it `confirm_required` before the space hears it, from the destructive actions of the space's own page data, and the registry holds the in-game menu's commands to the same rules (a command the space declares, a destructive one confirmed), answering the menu at once; the fixture space does too. **A page joins in its increment** (§4.2.2): a page whose `increment` is later than its space's (as the space registered it; GraceUI's own is its version's first two numbers) is absent from the rail (a category left empty goes too), Page Up and Page Down, search, related links and shortcuts, in game (`PageView::set_increment`) and in the browser (`withinIncrement`, where the page data arrives); a page with no increment, such as a later-version statement page, stays. The category icon and the slider's typed entry stay for later | Hiding later pages in the page data each space sends (every space would carry the rule); the space checking its own confirmations (a mod could forget, and the rule is the framework's) | One rule, kept by GraceUI for every space, the same in both views | S11.2.k; §4.2.2, §4.2.4, §4.7, §6.7, §7.3; D166, D171 |

no path, no name, nothing typed but its length), each line stamped with QueryPerformanceCounter in ms, the clock of EldenNet's recordings and the launcher's Marks. **New counters**, appended to the test structs: input of a device the holder takes that still reached the game (presses, releases, characters, mouse, raw input; Alt+F4's press left out), DirectInput and XInput reads GraceUI changed while a holder held and with none, and Present's prologue at install (D86's cue, now a log line in every build when it is a jump). **Another overlay**: the Steam overlay is reported as loaded or absent, by its module's fixed name only; | D176 | A holder's events through ABI 1 | Proposal 0003, agreed with EldenNet's session on 2026-11-20 for its chat box (its DESIGN §8.1 and NPC §5.5 to §5.7), with a fifth kind it asked for: D142's promise kept. **Opt in per request**: `GraceUiInputRequest`'s `reserved` word becomes `flags`, and with `GRACEUI_INPUT_EVENTS` the holder's input comes to its mod through `poll`; a request with 0, as every ABI 1.0 mod's, changes nothing, and an event struct of 1.0's size never gets the new kinds. **Five kinds**, only for the mod that holds input and only while it holds it (the holder's generation): `KEY` (the virtual key, `GRACEUI_MOD_*`, press or release, an auto-repeat marked), `CHARACTER` (one Unicode scalar: a surrogate pair joined, an ANSI window's bytes decoded, the IME's committed text, the Steam floating keyboard's), `POINTER` (while the holder takes the mouse: moves coalesced to the newest, buttons, the wheel, in the panel's own pixels, D137), `PAD` (while it takes the pad: an XInput button pressed or released, the pad's index; no sticks or triggers; the closing press masked from the game until released, invariant 8) and `INPUT_ENDED` (`why`, `GRACEUI_CLOSED_*`: released, menu key, danger, loading, focus lost, no render, fault, player), once and last, the holder's events not yet polled discarded before it. Each carries `dropped` (a ring of 256 a holder; the newest are dropped, never reordered). GraceUI keeps the menu key; a single Esc reaches the mod as a key, while Esc held 1 s or the menu key twice is the player's exit (invariant 17); Alt+F4 reaches the game; hotkeys stand aside while a holder has input (D77). **`set_caret`**, a slot at the table's end: the caret in the holder's panel's pixels, where GraceUI places the OS IME's composition and candidate windows (§5.6). **Not GraceUI's**: the clipboard (its chords come as keys; GraceUI never reads it), opening the Steam floating keyboard, a pad's sticks. `graceui_imgui.hpp` gains `feed` for a mod's own headless Dear ImGui. What is typed goes from the window procedure to the holder's mod alone, never logged or stored by GraceUI outside a test build's count (invariant 5). ABI 1.1, `has_cap("holder-events")`, in GraceUI 0.2.1 (a patch number: GraceUI's minor follows its increments, §10); EldenNet's v0.2 asks for floor 0.2.1 and checks the capability at connect. Later, when a mod needs them: telling a refused mod when input is free (D77), and a hotkey that is a pad chord with its own masking (EldenNet's View + D-pad Up, for S4's second round) (As built, 2026-11-20: ABI 1's append-only rule keeps `reserved`, so `flags` and a second reserved word are appended instead, making the request 32 bytes and the event 104, with `set_caret` the table's 21st slot at 176; a pointer event's `button_event` tells a button's press or release from a move or the wheel; a mod that does not hold input has its `set_caret` answered `ok` and ignored, and a mod's caret ends with its holder; a holder that another thread ends between its grant and its events' start still gets its end, once; graceui-sys's poll without a buffer asks at 1.1's size where GraceUI has the capability, a space's frame still waiting for a poll with one; the test build gains `GraceUI_Test_Pad`, a pad's state through the XInput detour's own filter, since the harness has no pad; the spike observer's typing test asks for its events and counts what its panel received, judged by `report.py`'s `panel_received`) | GraceUI feeding the mod's Dear ImGui context (a context across the ABI, invariant 16); a text-field widget of GraceUI's for mods' panels (a panel is the mod's own draw lists, D76); characters only; characters batched as UTF-8 | D142's promise, opt-in so no 1.0 mod changes, and one rule for every holder | S11.4.c; §5.6; D57, D76, D77, D137, D142, D155, D169; invariants 5, 8, 16, 17; proposal 0003 |
| D177 | The menu's default size, and a slider's margins | From the owner's look review of the in-game menu (S11.2.d, its first answers, 2026-11-20): "make the default size larger". `ui.scale` defaults to 1.25 instead of 1.0, its bounds (0.75 to 1.5) and step unchanged, so the largest effective scale, and with it the atlas's bound (§3.16), are as before; one setting still serves both views (§3.13), so the browser view is a quarter larger by default too, and so is EldenNet's text that follows the scale (its subtitles). The menu's faces are baked at warm-up at the store's value, the description's default until the menu has read it (no longer a fixed 1.0), and the test build reports that scale. | D181 | Quiet notices, a switch per mod | G32 (a) as decided, in GraceUI 0.2.3 (no change of the ABI). **The page**: Installed mods (`graceui.mods`) joins in 0.2: a table of the mods connected this session (Mod, Version, Uses from their caps in words, Notices) and a second, Quiet notices (`graceui.notices`), of what quiet mods posted, newest first. Both are topics of GraceUI's own space, built on GraceUI's thread at every wake from the consumers and the quiet store and published only when they change; a mod's connect and disconnect wake that thread. **The switch** is a table column with a `switch` (its command, the row's key field, the words for on and off), in both views: the view shows the word the space published and sends `mods.notices` `{"id", "on"}` when it is turned (Enter, or ← and →, in game; a click or the arrows in the browser), never flipping it itself (invariant 1); a refusal's reason shows under its row. (Amended by D184, 2026-11-20: a table with no body yet says "Nothing to show yet.", the browser's words.) **Kept**: the quiet ids go in `user\settings.json` as `"quiet_mods"` (ids only, and only when there are some), written when they change and read at load with the settings; the kept notices are for the session. With it, GraceUI's thread now also keeps a settings change from a browser while the menu is closed, and tells the mods of a new `ui.scale` then (D179, D180, which only did so when the menu next opened) | A setting per mod in the settings store (its keys would change with the mods connected); one switch for every mod (G32's (b)); dropping a quiet mod's notices unseen (the player could not read what they missed); telling the mod (a mod could work around it) | The player quiets the one mod that bothers them; GraceUI's own notices, which say how to take the controls back, always show | G32; D79, D134, D141, D169, D173, D179; invariants 1, 2, 3, 12; the harness's quiet scenarios |
| D182 | The menu by mouse | §5.5 built for the in-game menu, in GraceUI 0.2.4 (no change of the ABI), from the roadmap with no decision of the owner's needed. **Where**: each layout records where every focusable element was drawn (the rail's entries, search, the page's actions, tabs, section labels, rows, a setting's or a table switch's control, a slider's track, a choice's segments, Advanced and Related), the page's within its clip, so that a part scrolled out of view takes nothing. **What a press does**: it focuses the element under it and does what Enter does there (the rail opens a category or a page, a section opens or closes, a shortcut or a related page goes there, an action runs, a destructive one asking first); on a control, what a click on its part does: a switch turns, a choice takes the segment under the pointer, a slider the value under it (snapped to its step, within its bounds) and then follows the held button's moves until the release; a field (a key, a text, a secret) is focused only, edited in the browser view until GraceUI's own field. Only the left button acts. **The wheel** scrolls the page three rows a notch; the page then stays where the wheel left it until a key moves focus, when it follows focus again. **Hover**: `tint.hover` under the row, rail entry or button the pointer is over (§3.8's hover), only while the pointer is over the menu and no slider is held. **The path**: the window procedure's mouse messages for the menu holder go, as every holder's do, to the present thread, which feeds Dear ImGui (the Grace pointer, D178) and forwards the same events, in the same pixels, to GraceUI's thread through a ring of its own (moves one a frame), so a press lands where the pointer is drawn; GraceUI's thread runs the page view's `pointer` and lays the page out again. Nothing reaches the game while the menu holds the mouse (§5.2) | Hit-testing on the present thread (it would read the layout GraceUI's thread writes); the window procedure's own coordinates (they are the client area's, and the pointer is drawn in the back buffer's); hover-only actions (§5.5: nothing is) | The player can use the menu as they use the browser view; the present thread's work stays a ring push | §5.5, §3.8; D77, D167, D178; invariants 7, 8, 10; the harness's menu-mouse scenario |
| D183 | The security review's fixes, before testers get v0.2 | An independent read-only review of grace-serve, the bridge and the browser view (2026-11-20; the owner's go-ahead, at EldenNet's session's suggestion), given the code and the rules only, probing a live grace-dev on loopback, found the wire layer sound (Host, Origin, Fetch Metadata, the cookie and the ticket, the CSP and headers, the static files, HTTP and WebSocket framing, the bridge's routing, the settings store, the web app's handling of text and secrets) and six faults higher up, fixed here in GraceUI 0.2.5 but the fifth. **1. An anti-cheat found later** (§7.2 item 1, D81, invariant 4): a provider's `GRACEUI_WORLD_ANTI_CHEAT` only logged, and the module re-check did not exist. Now either closes the browser view's server for the rest of the session, whatever any space asks, with one log line; the re-check runs every 60 s while the server runs (the module clause; the parent cannot change); the menu stays. **2. A destructive action without its `confirm` text** ran on its first press in both views: `register_pages` now refuses one (the page lint's rule, now the framework's), and both views ask for the second press on the destructive tone whatever the text, with "This cannot be undone." when there is none. **3. GraceUI's own id**: a mod could register the space `graceui` and take GraceUI's place in the browser; it is reserved, and a registration the server does not take is refused rather than answered `ok`. **4. A third WebSocket** superseded the oldest view at its upgrade, before showing a ticket: now only at its welcome (§6.8). **5. Which view sent a command** (§7.3) is not told to the space yet: both views are this PC's until pairing (G9), and it is an addition to ABI 1 for EldenNet's session and the owner before the home network comes (§7.2 item 16); not built. **6. A space's `settings` topic** is now held to its registration: a description other than the registered one (compared decoded) is refused, a secret's value is replaced by whether it is set (`"set"` or `""`) with one log line a space, and a space may not declare a topic named `pages`, which is the server's own (D169). Also noted: `key_name` settings take any key word, `Escape` included; when `hotkeys.menu` is bound, it is checked against the keys that may be bound, so the player's exit stays uncapturable (invariants 8, 17) | Leaving 1 to the provider's own withdrawal (a second browser space kept the socket open); trusting the page lint for 2 (mods may hand-write page data, G18); a reserved prefix for 3 (one id is enough) | The rules as §7 states them, kept by the framework for every space | §6.8, §7.2, §7.3; D81, D88, D169, D173; invariants 3, 4, 5, 12; the harness's anti-cheat-flag and anti-cheat-recheck scenarios; grace-serve's and grace-spaces' tests |

| D203 | The browser view paces every frame it sends | EldenNet's fifth pages slice (2026-11-21): the server counts every frame of a connection against 20 a second and drops the rest unread (§6.8), but the browser view paced only its commands and sent each sub and unsub at once; a page reading many topics right after connecting (EldenNet's AI › Models reads 14) could lose a sub, leaving a picker empty or a card without its status until the page was opened again. The web transport now sends subs, unsubs and commands through one queue, in order, at 16 a second (the server's limit less a margin for frames that arrive closer than they were sent); hello and pongs go at once and count too; an unsub of a sub still waiting takes it back, so a quick page change costs nothing. The server's limit is unchanged. In GraceUI 0.2.13; no change of the protocol or the ABI | Counting subs apart from the limit (weakens §6.8's bound); a picker's `choices_from` taking a member, to need fewer topics | The limit is a safety bound; the page keeps to it | EldenNet's request of 2026-11-21 |
| D204 | Pad chords for mods' hotkeys | G35 as decided, built in GraceUI 0.2.14 as ABI 1.3: `pad_chord` is `GRACEUI_PAD_CHORD(anchor, key)`, the anchor's button in the high 16 bits and the key's in the low, each one of XInput's fourteen buttons (no trigger or stick), the two different; `has_cap("pad-chords")`. `grace-input`'s `PadChords` runs on XInput's reads after the pad ledger: the anchor always reaches the game; a key pressed 150 ms or more after it is held back; both held 0.4 s from the key's press fire one `GRACEUI_EVENT_HOTKEY`, the key never reaches the game, and both are hidden until each is released, the packet number changed; either released sooner replays the key as one tap (two of the game's reads and 50 ms, the packet number changed at its press and its release), and a key the tap leaves held stays hidden until released, so the game sees one press. Chords stand aside while a holder has input (a chord resolving then is dropped with no tap: the game sees the pad at rest) and whenever `request_input` would answer `not_now`, or in the notice-only mode (no chord starts; one resolving fails at once, with its tap). Each pad's state has one reader at a time, under a try-lock: another reader meanwhile never waits and gives the game what the last read decided. A chord another hotkey has bound registers unbound, with a log line; a hotkey's key and its chord are bound each on its own and either fires it, and `GRACEUI_UNBOUND` answers when any default it names is taken. A mod connected with ABI minor 2 or less still gets `not_yet` for a chord | A field of its own for the order; a bit mask; holding View back whenever the anchor is held; `refused` for an older mod's chord | Proposal 0005 §2 to §4 as decided; a mod built before 1.3 was told `not_yet` and retried without the chord, and could take `refused` as final | G35; proposal 0005; EldenNet NPC §5.6, O11 |

What only a real pad shows (the three estimates of D204, that D-pad Up then View is free in the game, a tap's effect on the game's map, the Deck's and Proton's checks) is for testers' machines, in S4's second round and EldenNet's matrix, and never a step that waits on the owner; until then those figures stay EST | Waiting for the owner to test on a pad | The owner tests with keyboard and mouse only, with no pad, VR set or handheld (2026-11-22) | G35's note of 2026-11-22; §10 |
