| D207 | A holder's end is never lost | Found by the TSan tree's run under load (2026-11-22): when a mod's box of a holder's events (D176) is full, `end()` empties it and pushes the end again, and that push could still find the ring full while another thread's pop had claimed its slot and not yet given it back, so the end was dropped and the mod never heard that its holder ended. Now an end the emptied ring still refuses waits in the box's own slot, which `take()` gives before anything in the ring; nothing is reordered, since whatever the ring holds of an ended holder is discarded anyway. The test that found it read its "ended" flag after taking, racing its other taker; it reads it before taking now | Retrying the push until it succeeds, which waits on another thread | A mod that never hears its end keeps its own view as if it held input (invariant 17's honesty); the box never waits (D176) | D176; the holder-mail stress test |
| D208 | Rebinding hotkeys, on GraceUI's Hotkeys page | G37 as decided: every mod's hotkey is changed on GraceUI › Hotkeys. Each row (its id "‹mod id›/‹action›") offers Change key, Change pad chord and Reset; the page's action Reset every hotkey (destructive, with its words) puts every one back. `hotkeys.key`, `hotkeys.chord`, `hotkeys.reset` and `hotkeys.reset_all`, GraceUI's own commands, take `{"id", "value", "confirm"}`: a key or chord another bound hotkey has answers `confirm_required` with words ("F9 is Tide Charts's Show the tide table. Use it here, and leave that one with no key?"), and with `confirm` it is moved, the other left with none; the menu's key is never moved from it (refused in words); Escape, Alt+F4, the modifiers and the Windows keys are refused. `grace-input`'s table keeps each hotkey's defaults for a reset, and a fixed store (256, EST) keeps the player's bindings by mod and action, written to GraceUI's settings file as `hotkey_bindings` (ids, key names and chords only), applied at each registration whether or not the mod is installed now, and to hotkeys a mod registered before GraceUI's thread read the file (each kept one freed first, so a key one gives up is free for another). The one that lost a key is kept with none, so the next session's order of registration cannot undo the move. `hotkeys.menu` is live: one key alone (no Ctrl, Shift or Alt; not Escape, a modifier or a Windows key, refused in words before the store takes it), applied at once in the window procedure and DirectInput's view; a mod's hotkey on the new key loses it, kept so once the player moved it | Each mod's own page (D115 as written); refusing a taken key | One home for every binding (invariant 12) and every conflict in one list; mods with no page served | G37; proposal 0009; D115 |
| D209 | Capture before a row action (protocol 1.4) | A row action may set `capture`, `key` or `chord`: both views take it from the player first and send it as `value`, with the row's key; a `confirm_required` answer with words is asked (the browser's confirm dialog, the game's modal with Enter) and sent again with `confirm`. In game a small modal over the page takes the next key press whole (the window procedure hands it to the menu with its Ctrl, Shift and Alt while a capture waits, never as one of the menu's keys); Escape or B cancels; on a row's capture Backspace leaves it with no key; a pad chord is chosen with two pickers (↑ ↓, ← →, Enter; R for none), as the browser's dialog does with two lists (it has no pad, §5.9). A key's setting (`key_name`, such as `hotkeys.menu`) is captured the same way in game. Words wrap in the card; its keys are drawn in it, the footer's being under its scrim; the menu's close routine cancels an open capture. A row whose key cell is empty takes no row actions, in either view (the menu's own row on the Hotkeys page). Key names in the browser follow D206, six codes shortened to fit | A capture of the chord held on a pad (the owner has none, D205; testers later) | The chord's buttons are named, not felt; one capture path for every space's row actions | G37; proposal 0009 §3 |
| D210 | A mod reads its hotkey's binding (ABI 1.4) | `describe_hotkey(consumer, id, GraceUiHotkeyBinding*)`, appended to the table, fills which of its key and chord fire it now, the key as a virtual key with its modifiers, the chord as `GRACEUI_PAD_CHORD`, and both as the Hotkeys page writes them ("Ctrl+K", "D-pad Up, then View"); `_REFUSED` for an id not the mod's or a struct below 1.4's size; `has_cap("hotkey-bindings")`. `GRACEUI_EVENT_HOTKEY_CHANGED` (`hotkey_id`) tells a mod connected with ABI minor 4 or more that the player changed one, after its presses; an older mod is never sent the kind | Nothing, a mod wording its text without the key | EldenNet's call-out says "Press T to talk" with whatever key the player chose | G37 (4) (a); proposal 0009 §5 |
| D211 | Hotkey bindings off game | In game only for now: a hotkey exists only as a mod's registration in game, with its name and defaults, and a host outside the game (EldenNet's launcher, G29) serves spaces through `grace-serve` but holds neither GraceUI's own space nor its hotkey table. The bindings are on EldenNet's O77 short list. A later proposal may show and change the kept bindings off game, if GraceUI's settings file keeps each hotkey's last registered name and defaults | The launcher's host serving GraceUI › Hotkeys from the settings file | No registration to name a binding off game, and nothing EldenNet needs there | EldenNet's question, 2026-11-22; proposal 0009 §9; G29 |
| D212 | A wait handle for each mod's events (ABI 1.5) | D71's optional event handle, built for EldenNet's chat box (its opening within 50 ms, NPC §5.1): `wake_event(consumer, void** event)`, appended to the table, `has_cap("wake")`, gives the mod a Win32 handle of its own (SYNCHRONIZE access) to an auto-reset event, one per mod number, made at the first ask and never closed while GraceUI runs, so that a thread that read it can always set it; a mod's disconnect unsets it for the number's next mod. GraceUI sets it whenever something is kept for the mod's poll: a hotkey's press (the window procedure; a completed chord, in XInput's reads), a hotkey's change (GraceUI's thread), a holder's event (whichever thread keeps it), a view's watch, unwatch or command (the menu's, and the browser's on the server's I/O thread, through each space's wake), the display event (whichever thread changes it); and once when it is asked for. Setting it never waits and calls no mod code; the registry's and the holder mail's hooks are plain function pointers taking no lock. A mod waits on it, polls until `GRACEUI_EVENT_NONE`, then waits again; a wake with nothing to poll can happen. In the harness under Wine the waiting thread woke 1 to 5 ms after a hotkey's key was posted, under 1 ms after a holder's key, about 2.5 ms after a resize | A callback on GraceUI's thread; a handle the mod gives GraceUI, which it might close while GraceUI sets it | No mod code on GraceUI's threads (D71); an event GraceUI owns cannot be closed under it | D71; EldenNet's question, 2026-11-22 (its T6, NPC §5.1) |
| D213 | The profile editor, the one content editor in v1 (EldenNet's S7.2.a) | EldenNet's O43 (the owner's, 2026-11-07) puts the profile editor in v1, at EldenNet v0.6, in both views: the player's own notes on a persona, a card or a shipped character, their own character profiles for people met, and the Tarnished's own bio (O46). D2's "no content editors in v1" stands for everything else (personas, cards, knowledge, triggers, prompts), and Content packs stays read-only (D46). Since G20 (a) the editor's pages, fields, checks, commands and draft helper are EldenNet's (its PAGES, NPC §4.7; S7.2.b), and so are the rules of the needs list that became EldenNet's (SP1's `subjects` and the player's own text, SP4's people met, PV4's draft text): GraceUI's part is the components, put to the owner as G38 with proposal 0010. The needs list of 2026-11-07 was brought up to date with O46 (the five-layer bio, the Tarnished's own) and O86 (no checks against fixture states) in proposal 0010 §2 | Keeping D2 whole, the editor waiting for Workshop's editors (Later) | O43 is the owner's; the editor is the player's own text, not shipped content | EldenNet's O43, O46, O86; G20 (a); G38; proposal 0010; profile-editor-needs.md |
| D214 | GraceUI's own text field in game (G38 (1) (a), GraceUI 0.2.18) | `grace-view`'s `TextField`, pure: its text in UTF-8, a caret and a selection by code point; typing over a selection; Backspace and Delete, by word with Ctrl; ← and → (by word with Ctrl, selecting with Shift), Home and End; Ctrl+A, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+X and Ctrl+V. One paragraph (G38 (3)): a control character typed is ignored, one pasted becomes a space, invalid UTF-8 is dropped; a field's room is in bytes and never takes a character by halves; it wraps after spaces (a word wider than the line broken between characters) with ↑ and ↓ by line, for the editor's prose. **The page view edits one field at a time**: a text setting with no choices, sent on Enter if it changed; a secret setting and a table's row secret (D194), masked, drawn as discs so that no glyph of a secret is laid out, empty at the start, never copied, overwritten when they end, the view keeping only whether a secret is set (invariant 5); a form's fields (D195), Enter moving on to the next field and then its button, the text kept in the view until the space accepts the form, which empties it (G38 (2)); and search (`/` or Ctrl+F, §4.3): this space's settings, eight results, Enter opening the row on its tab with its section and Advanced disclosure opened (shortcut rows now do the same; on a page with tabs they did not). Enter, A or a click starts an edit; Enter keeps it, Tab keeps it and moves on, Escape or B leaves (a setting's and a secret's edit dropped, a form field keeping its text); a click in the field moves the caret, one elsewhere keeps a text's or a form field's edit; the close routine ends an edit as Escape does. While a field edits every key is the field's (Q, E and R type): the host hands GraceUI's thread its editing keys and every character in order with them, so that none is lost to an edit not yet begun, and that thread drops the characters no field wants without laying the page out again. Ctrl+V reads the clipboard's Unicode text, at most 16 KiB, on the window procedure's thread; Ctrl+C and Ctrl+X write it from GraceUI's thread, the game's window as its owner. The caret is steady (invariant 14) and is the IME's place (§5.6). The "in the browser view" lines of forms, row secrets and text settings went. **Not built:** Steam's floating keyboard on a pad (§5.6; for testers' pads, D205), the IME's composition drawn inside the field (D57's later work), grapheme clusters (a combining mark is a step of its own), and search across spaces in game | ImGui's `InputText` (D57); the fields left to the browser view until 0.6 (G38 (1) (b)) | One field for every kind, so that no page's text waits for the browser; the owner's G38 (1) (a) | §4.3, §5.3, §5.6; D57, D194, D195; G38; proposal 0010 §3.5; the typing scenario |
| D215 | Topic families and master–detail (G38 (1) (a); protocol 1.5, GraceUI 0.2.19) | **Families.** A state topic may be declared `family = true`. Its members, `<family>/<key>`, are watched and published one by one as state topics of their own, and the family itself never. A key is one or more of a-z, 0-9, '.', '_' and '-', and a member's whole id is at most 64 bytes. A space learns which members are watched through `WATCHED` and `UNWATCHED`, as for any topic (D169), and publishes a member while it is watched; a state for an unwatched member is taken and goes nowhere, and the family's own id is refused. The server holds at most 64 of a space's members watched at once (EST), each in a slot that the first view to watch it takes, given back once nobody watches it and the space has heard so: the space hears each member's watch and unwatch under its own id, and a slot's generation keeps a state published for one member from reaching the next. The registry and the menu take members as topics. **Master and detail.** A page may have a `master`: a state topic with a table's body (`columns`, `rows`), the column holding each row's key, the family whose member that key names, the list's label, and its own words for an empty list and for no row chosen. Its list is drawn at the left in both views (a card in game, a listbox in the browser, stacked when narrow), and the page's own tabs and sections at the right as the chosen row's detail. A component block bound to the family draws the chosen row's member, which the views watch with the page's other topics and let go when another row is chosen. The first row is chosen as soon as the list has one. A page keeps its chosen row for the session, never in the address, browser storage or a log. A row whose key names no member is not shown, and while no row is chosen the detail is not drawn (no focus stops). In game the rows are focus stops before the tabs: ↑ and ↓ move along the list, choosing as they go; → or Enter go into the detail; ← (where the control takes no ← →), ↑ from the detail's first stop, and Esc come back to the chosen row; a click chooses. In the browser, ↑, ↓, Home and End move the choice, and → or Enter go into the detail. The lint checks that a family is a state topic, that a master names a declared state topic and a family, that a family is drawn only on a page whose master names it, and that fixtures name members (`<family>/<key>`); grace-dev and the fixture transport serve members from them. The demo space's Lamps page uses it. Proposal 0010 §3.2 made master–detail a block; here it belongs to the page, so that the page's tabs and sections are the detail, as EldenNet's Named NPCs needs (a list, then Profile, Allowances and Reactions) *Dated note, 2026-11-22 (D217):* on a master page a block bound to any family of the space's, not only the master's `detail`, draws that family's member for the chosen row's key, and the lint accepts one there; an editor's document is one such member. | A master–detail block holding blocks of its own (proposal 0010 §3.2: nested blocks and nested tabs in the page data); topics declared one by one, a topic per NPC | One page, one list, its tabs as the detail. Members are topics of their own, so every rule a topic has holds for them: watched alone, published only while watched, a view's 32 | §2.5, §6.5, §6.8; D36, D169, D186; G38; proposal 0010 §3.1, §3.2; the master scenario |
| D216 | Slow commands (G38 (1) (a); protocol 1.6, GraceUI 0.2.20) | A space may name up to 8 of its own commands in its page data's `slow_commands`; settings commands are never slow. Such a command (EldenNet's `profile.draft`, a model's call of up to 60 s) is answered within `GRACEUI_SLOW_ANSWER_DEADLINE_MS`, 90 s, rather than the 10 s of D169, whether a browser or the menu sent it; past that GraceUI answers it `internal`, as for any command, and its log line says 90 s. The browser waits 95 s for its answer, the 90 s and a margin; the menu waits as long as the registry does. A slow command still counts once in a view's 20 a second and in the space's queue of 32, and other commands are not held behind it. In game, the control that sent one (a header or section action, a row action, a form's button) reads "Working…" and takes no press until the host says its answer came, whatever it was: one sender at a time per command, oldest first. In the browser, a form's button and a row's actions already wait while their command is in flight. The lint checks that a slow command is one of the space's own and named once. The demo space names its "Order oil" slow | The deadline per command in each result (a space's own say, at answer time); every command at 90 s | A deadline the space states in its page data, checked at registration, so that GraceUI and every view know it before the command is sent | §6.6, §6.8; D169; G38; proposal 0010 §3.4; the slow scenario |
| D217 | The editor block (G38 (1) (a), its part d; protocol 1.7, GraceUI 0.2.21) | A block of kind `editor` on a state topic, or on a master page a family (whose member for the chosen row it draws), shows the document the space publishes there: `{target, rev, state: {word, tone, line}, draft_blocked, draft_help, fields}`, each field `{id, label, kind, help, value, reference, reference_label, max_words, max_chars, max_items, min, max, choices, choices_from, locked, nullable}`, an id dotted if the space likes, of the kinds `line`, `prose` (one paragraph, wrapped), `list` (items of words or `{text, choice, key, locked}`), `choice`, `pick` (choices from a topic, D193), `number`, `switch` and `shown` (read-only, never sent). Both views read it tolerantly: a field with no id, label or known kind, a second of one id, or a value of another kind is left out, never guessed at; `null` is "not set here", which is not empty, where `nullable` allows it. The player's edits are kept in the view's memory for the session by the document's topic, whatever page is open and through the menu's close (G38 (2)), never on disk or in browser storage, and marked Unsaved; the browser asks before its tab closes while any are held, and since nothing is lost by leaving a page, neither view asks then. The commands are the space's own, named in the block's `editor` {save, check, reset, restore, draft, notes_label, notes_max}, save alone required: check and save send `{target, rev, doc}`, `rev` the document's revision when the first unsaved edit was made and `doc` every field but a shown one, a locked field and a locked item as published and each item's key sent back; reset sends `{target, all: true, confirm: true}` once the player confirms it; restore `{target}`; draft `{target, notes}`, which must be one of the space's slow commands (D216). An answer's body may hold `messages`, `[{field, item, level, words}]`, each shown under its field or item, or for the whole file, in the space's words; a save over a revision the space has moved on from answers the new error code `conflict`, and the view says "Changed elsewhere since you opened it." and offers Reload, which asks first while edits are unsaved. A draft's answer, `{draft: {field: value}, notice}`, shows each proposal beside its field with Use this and Keep mine, and Use all at the top; a proposal for a field the document lacks, a shown or locked one, or of another kind is dropped; taken, it is an unsaved edit, and a draft lives while its page is open. GraceUI holds a field to its `max_chars`, a list to its `max_items`, the notes to `notes_max` (at most 400) and a number to its `min` and `max`, and counts words and characters live, the count in the warning tone past `max_words`; the space decides everything else at its one entry point (invariant 3). In game: Enter edits a line, a prose field or an item in GraceUI's own field (D214), held in characters as well as bytes, ↑ ↓ moving by line in prose and Enter ending it; ← → change a choice, a pick, a number (Shift by ten) or an item's choice, and Enter a switch; Shift+↑ ↓ move an item; R undoes a field's edit, then leaves a nullable field to the space, or removes an item; an item added and left empty goes again. Its answer comes back to the element that sent it, whatever is open. The demo space's Lamps page has a Your notes tab, an editor on `lantern.note`'s members, which its fixture space answers as a space would | One editor a page, or the document in the page data; GraceUI checking the limits itself; edits kept on disk | Proposal 0010 §3.3 as approved, with EldenNet's five additions (§7: an item's key, locked items, dotted ids, null as not set here, messages for the whole file); the document is the space's, so a view only shows it and sends the player's edits to its one entry point | §2.5, §5.6, §6.6; D193, D214, D215, D216; G38; proposal 0010 §3.3, §7; the editor scenario |
| D218 | A save's chosen row (EldenNet's WHY§387; GraceUI 0.2.22, protocol 1.7 unchanged) | A save's answer body may carry `chosen: "<key>"`, a key of the page's master list (a key of its `detail` family's members, as D215's). The view that saved, which alone gets the answer's body (D169), takes it as the page's chosen row once the list has that row: at once if it does, else when the list's next state brings it, unless the player chooses a row by hand first (a key or a press that leaves focus on the list). A key that names no member, an answer that is not ok, or a page with no master list: nothing changes. Focus stays where the player left it: on Save, say, once the new row's member draws it again, unless a key or a press has moved focus since; in the browser likewise, and a view never draws a topic's body for a render under another topic's name once a block's topic changes. EldenNet sends it when a profile written for a `named.<param>` row turns that row into `mine.<id>` (its NPC §4.7) | The first row, as D215 chooses when the chosen row goes; a column naming each row's successor | The space knows which row follows; a list alone cannot tell a renamed row from a removed one | D215, D217; EldenNet's WHY§387; the editor scenario |
| D219 | Search across spaces in game (§4.3; brought forward from 0.4, GraceUI 0.2.23) | The menu's search field finds the settings of every space the menu shows, not only the open space's: each space's page view is linked to the others' (by the host, anew whenever the spaces change) and adds their hits to its own, each named by its space ("GraceUI › Menu" beside a hit of another space's, its home page's name alone beside one of its own). | D220 | The init's step, readable from outside (EldenNet's spike package case A2; GraceUI 0.2.24) | An init that stalls left no trace: GraceUI's log is drained by the init thread itself, which becomes GraceUI's own thread, so a thread stuck before the install said nothing, and the state read only `started` (A2 under load: injected late, `install_runs` 0 for 240 s; it passed three times alone). The init thread now sets a step before each of its parts: 1 its fonts, 2 waiting for the game's window, 3 binding Direct3D, 4 reading the hook targets from a swap chain of its own, 5 the input targets, 6 the install, 7 hooked (set before the host reads as hooked; a give-up leaves its step). The test build gives it as `GraceUI_Test_InitStep` (no struct grows, so a vendored `graceui_test.h` still reads `GraceUiTestState`); the spike observer writes it in each state line as `init_step` (left out by an older test build), and `report.py` names the step in the hooks row's detail when the host never hooked. The harness checks step 7 wherever it checks the hooks, and the spike scenario waits, within 600 frames, for a state line with a frame drawn, as a slow machine needs | A watchdog that gives up after a time (it would hide where the init stalls, and a slow machine is not a fault); a log file of the init's own | A stall the reports can place is one a later run can find; the release DLL pays an atomic store per part, once | D71, D120, D175; EldenNet's WHY§255; the spike scenario |
| D221 | Other devices at home, the server's side (G9, §7.2 items 16 and 25; GraceUI 0.2.25, protocol 1.8) | With `dashboard.lan` on, `grace-serve` also listens on the PC's private IPv4 addresses (RFC 1918), at most four, each bound by name on the browser view's port, never 0.0.0.0 or a public, multicast or reserved address; it lists them again every 10 s and follows a change (every device connection closes then), and tries a bind that failed again only once the addresses change. It takes peers from private, link-local or loopback addresses alone, and at most 8 of the 16 HTTP connections. An unpaired device gets the built-in pairing page for any page load, with its own `/pair.js` and `/pair.css` (the CSP allows no inline script and no form post), and `POST /api/pair` (Origin and Fetch Metadata checked, JSON `{code, name}`, 256 bytes at most); anything else is 404, and a ticket or a WebSocket 403. The code is compared in constant time under the server's lock: right, it pairs once and is void; five wrong ones void it, but one peer's are counted only up to three, past which that peer is refused even the right code while the code stays for another device; a full list (16 devices), a bad name or a bad body spends nothing; and a wrong code and none showing get one answer, so that the network cannot see when a code shows. A device's view, waiting socket or ticket never takes this PC's place in the shared caps (§6.8): this PC's take a device's first. A paired device gets `en_device` (32 random bytes in base64url; `Path=/`, 400 days, renewed at each page load; `HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict`, host-only), of which the server keeps only the SHA-256, compared with every device's in full; its tickets open a WebSocket on the network listener alone and for that device alone, and the session cookie is never granted or taken there. A paired device is refused, with `not_now` and "Only on the PC the game runs on.", before any confirmation is asked, every command its space marks sensitive: protocol 1.8's `sensitive_commands` on a space's pages and `sensitive` on a setting (its `settings.set` or key reset, the reset of a page that holds one, a reset of all), and, implicitly, every secret setting and row-secret command. Forget closes the device's connections at once (its WebSocket with `unauthorised`, so that its page reloads to the pairing page) and voids its tickets; switching off closes the listeners and every device connection (`shutdown`). The log carries counts only, never an address, a code, a cookie or a device's name | Every interface with a peer filter; IPv6 at once; the session cookie on both listeners; a code a browser shows | Bound by name, the PC's public side stays shut whatever the firewall says; a cookie per device is revoked alone; a hash leaves nothing worth taking from the file; each space marks its own sensitive commands (D81). IPv4 alone for now: a phone at home has one on every router; private IPv6 (ULA) can follow | G9, D66, D81, D113; `network_tests`, the pairing scenario |
| D222 | Pair a device, in game (G9, D113; GraceUI 0.2.25) | GraceUI › Browser view's Other devices section holds `dashboard.lan` (live, off by default; its help explains Windows' firewall prompt before it is first turned on, D113), a section action, Pair a device (`dashboard.pair`, no arguments), and the paired devices' table (`graceui.devices`: name, date paired, connected or not), each row with a destructive Forget (`dashboard.forget_device`, `{id}`). Pair a device opens a sheet over the menu (D223): the code as two groups of three digits, the address to open (`http://ADDRESS:PORT/`) and the time left. The code is valid while the sheet stays open: its Close, or any close of the menu, danger's included, voids it at once; when a device pairs, the sheet names it. It is refused, with the reason, while Other devices is off, the browser view is not served, the PC has no private address, or sixteen devices are paired. A device's name is typed on its own pairing page (1 to 32 characters), not in game: the phone's keyboard is to hand, and only the code is read in game (amending §6.6's table, which gave `dashboard.pair` the name). `dashboard.pair` is refused from every browser (`not_now`); it, `dashboard.forget_device`, `dashboard.disconnect`, the three `dashboard.*` settings, `hotkeys.menu`, the `hotkeys.*` commands and `mods.notices` are marked sensitive, so a paired device is refused them while this PC's browser may use them. Devices are kept in `Game\GraceUI\user\devices.toml` (a version line, then each device's id, name, local date and the cookie's SHA-256 in hex; written whole and swapped in, as `settings.json` is): a file not of that shape is left unread, and no device is paired; an entry that breaks a rule is left out. The Browser view's list says "On: served on this PC's home network too" and "Devices connected: N of M paired", never the PC's network address, which only the sheet shows, in game (PV3) | The name typed in game (GraceUI's text field, D57) before the code; the address on the page in both views; devices in `settings.json` | The code is the secret and shows only where the player is; an address in a topic reaches every browser, a paired phone's too; a file of their own keeps devices out of Copy settings and the bug report (§7.2 item 16) | G9, D66, D113, D179; the pairing scenario |

The host shows it again whenever its words change (each second, for the code's time) and closes it itself in the menu's close routine, which answers nothing. A mod reads it in `GRACEUI_EVENT_COMMAND`'s frame; GraceUI's own space gets it too. Nothing else changes: GraceUI already refuses a paired device what a space marks sensitive (D221), so a space needs it only for rules of its own, such as wording or a log line | A view field the space must trust; a separate event kind per view | §7.3's rule as written: the actor is the channel, never the view's word | D183, D221; proposal 0007 §5; `network_tests`, `spaces_tests` |
| D225 | The pairing's security review (2026-11-23; GraceUI 0.2.25) | One read-only reviewer, given the code and the rules and not our conclusions (WHY§264's method), found no way past the pairing page for an unpaired device or a hostile page, and found the session cookie, Host and Origin, tickets, Forget, binding, lifetimes, threads, `devices.toml` and the log sound. Fixed before release: one peer could void each code with five posts and flood the log (now three tries a peer, one answer for wrong and none, the log line at most once in 10 s with its count); a device could rebind the menu's key and the hotkeys or quiet mods (now sensitive, D222); a device's view, waiting socket or ticket could take this PC's place (no longer, D221); the code's body shares the ticket's buffer (now a compile-time check); a device's name could hide characters to look like another's (zero-width, bidirectional, C1, soft hyphen, separators and BOM refused). Accepted, and said here: a device's cookie is a bearer token over plain HTTP on the home network, so a device on the same network that can read or redirect its traffic (ARP spoofing, or taking the PC's old address after a new lease) could replay it until the device is forgotten; rotating it at each load was set aside, as it breaks a second tab and writes the file at every load, and a phone at home on plain HTTP is what G9 decided (§7.2 item 25). The listener takes the PC's first four private addresses in Windows' order, a VPN's or a virtual switch's among them, and the sheet lists each, so the player can choose | A new cookie at each page load; the adapter of the default route alone (a new import, `iphlpapi`) | A review of the joins finds what each module's tests do not; what is accepted is written down where the next reader looks | WHY§264; D221, D222; `pairing_tests`, `network_tests`, the pairing scenario |
| D226 | Uploads, the wire (G28, §7.2 item 18; GraceUI 0.2.26, protocol 1.9) | A space names its file commands in its page data, `file_commands` (at most four), each one of its commands, marked sensitive, not slow and not a settings command (the page lint). The page sends `upload` (the command's id, name and args, the file's size and its SHA-256 in lowercase hex) and gets `upload_ticket` (that id and a one-use ticket) or, refused, the command's `result`: a device (`not_now`), a command that takes no file (`unknown_command`), an empty file or one over 32 MiB (`invalid_value`), a hash not in 64 lowercase hex (`bad_message`), a second upload of the session's (`busy`), or all four ticket slots in use (`busy`). The ticket is bound to the session, the space, the command and args, the size and the hash, and lasts 60 s. `POST /upload`, on the loopback listener alone, needs every check of §7.2, `Content-Type: application/octet-stream`, a `Content-Length` (no `Transfer-Encoding`) and the ticket in `Grace-Upload-Ticket`, which is spent as soon as it matches. A length over the ticket's gets 413 before a byte is read, one under it 400; a bad or spent ticket 403; no length 411; another type 415; the body has 30 s. GraceUI's worker thread hashes the bytes; a hash that differs gets 400; a session gone meanwhile 409; then 202, and the bytes go to the space with the command, its `result` coming back by the command's id as for a `cmd` (§6.7); each failure after the ticket also answers that id in words. The bytes live in a buffer that zeroes itself when the last holder lets it go; no file name is sent or used. A `cmd` naming a file command is refused (`invalid_value`, "This takes a file: add it with its button."). The network listener has no such route (its body cap answers 413). `welcome` now names the view, `browser` or `device`, so the page offers the upload only where it can be sent | A ticket in the URL (it would reach logs and history); the bytes in WebSocket frames (§6.8's 16 KiB); hashing on the loop thread (32 MiB would stall every other page); a multipart form (a file name and a parser for nothing) | The narrowest route that does what G28 decided: one header more than an API call, one body, one answer, and nothing kept | G28; D114; `upload_tests`, `bridge_tests`, `network_tests` |
| D227 | The upload block (G28; GraceUI 0.2.26) | A section's block of kind `upload` names one of the space's file commands, its button's label (`submit`) and, optionally, the kinds it takes (`accept`, extensions such as ".toml, .txt", checked by the lint). In the browser on this PC it is a drop zone with that button: one file at a time, its kind checked there, its name shown there and never sent, then "Sending …", "Accepted: …" or the refusal's words under it, as a form's are (D195). On a paired device, or wherever the page is not a secure context, it says "only on the PC the game runs on" and offers nothing to send. In game it is a line, "<label>: in the browser view on this PC.", since a file is chosen in a browser. On a greyed page it does nothing (D188). The demo space's Keepers page has one, a roster file whose keepers it counts | A file picker in game (a Windows dialog over a full-screen game, and a path the player types); a hidden zone on a device | The player sees where the file goes and why a phone cannot send one; the page data stays the one source (invariant 12) | G28, D195, D226; the DOM test, `view_tests`, the pages scenario |
| D228 | Files to mods through the ABI (G28; ABI 1.6, GraceUI 0.2.26) | `GraceUiEvent` grows, append-only, by `file`, `file_capacity` and `file_length` (160 bytes); `GRACEUI_UPLOAD_MAX` is 33 554 432. A command that came with a file arrives as `GRACEUI_EVENT_COMMAND` with its bytes copied into the mod's buffer and their length; a buffer too small answers `FULL` with both lengths set and keeps the event, so a second poll with a larger buffer takes it whole. A mod whose event struct is older than 1.6 never gets a file: GraceUI answers such a command `not_now` itself, and `register_pages` refuses `file_commands` from a mod built before ABI 1.6, and its log line says why. GraceUI lets its copy go once the mod has taken it, and the buffer zeroes itself | A pointer into GraceUI's memory (a lifetime across the ABI); a callback with the bytes (on the present thread, against invariant 10) | One copy into memory the mod owns, under the poll the mod already makes; nothing new to free | D73, D74, D212, D226; `spaces_tests`, the pages scenario |

Fixed before release: one session could queue 32 files of 32 MiB behind a slow mod, since its place is free once a file is handed over (now one file waits for a space at a time: a second gets 503 and `busy`, "Another file is waiting for this mod: add this one once it has answered."); the tail of each file stayed in the server's read buffer (now wiped after every read); the in-game menu could send a file command with no file (now refused, `invalid_value`, before the mod sees it, and a page's action, row action, row secret, form or editor naming one is refused at registration and by the lint); an event held on `FULL` outlived its deadline, so a file could reach the mod after the view was told it failed, and twice on a retry (now let go at its deadline); and an upload's result ignored a row secret's rule for its body (now as a `cmd`'s). Accepted, and said here: the ticket is a bearer token on loopback, so any local program holding both the session's cookie and the ticket could use it; it travels only on that session's WebSocket and dies with it, and "bound to the session" means its result goes there | A slot held until the mod has taken the file (a slow mod would block every other space's uploads); a byte cap on each space's queue | A review of the joins finds what each module's tests do not; what is accepted is written down where the next reader looks | WHY§264; D225, D226, D228; `upload_tests`, `spaces_tests` |
| D230 | The first frames' step, and GraceUI's log beside the spike report (EldenNet's spike package case A1; GraceUI 0.2.27) | A1 (GraceUI injected early, EldenNet and the stand-in after) fell twice with an access violation after GraceUI had bound the game's swap chain and before it drew, Present's prologue already holding a jump; the state lines, five seconds apart, could not say where. The test build now records how far GraceUI's first frames got, each step once and never undone: 1 a Present through GraceUI's detour, 2 the game's swap chain bound, 3 the present queue found, 4 the atlas asked of GraceUI's thread, 5 the atlas baked, 6 the renderer's objects made on the game's device, 7 Dear ImGui's backends begun, 8 the atlas's upload submitted, 9 the atlas on the GPU, 10 GraceUI's first frame submitted, 11 the game's Present returned after it (`GraceUI_Test_FrameStep`). The observer writes it in each state line, and a state line at once whenever it changes, so a game that falls in GraceUI's first frames leaves its last step within 50 ms; `report.py` names it in the drawing row when nothing was drawn. GraceUI's log, which goes to the debugger alone, is copied by the observer into `graceui-log.txt` beside the report (`GraceUI_Test_LogCount` and `GraceUI_Test_GetLogNumber`, by each line's number, so that no line still in the ring is missed or repeated): never into the report, whose words stay the observer's own, and holding GraceUI's own text, numbers and mods' ids, never a path or a name (PV3). | D231 | The spike observer's fault recorder (EldenNet's spike package case A1; GraceUI 0.2.28, test-only) | All four of A1's and A0's crashes, Windows' own Application Error events say, faulted with the instruction pointer at 0, in no module: a call or a jump through a null pointer, or a return to 0, which names no caller. grace-spike.dll now puts a vectored exception handler first in the process's list that records an access violation, an illegal or privileged instruction, an in-page error, a division by zero or (since 0.2.30, for spikes.4's two quits that ended in it) the heap's corruption, 0xC0000374, and never handles one (it answers "continue search" whatever it found): the code, the access, whether the target was the null page, GraceUI's frame step (D230), the thread, and the faulting thread's frames as a module and an offset, the instruction first and then, where there is no unwind data (the pointer at 0, a leaf, code made at run time), the return address at the stack's top, which names the call that went there, then up to a dozen unwound within the thread's own stack. The report gets a `fault` line whose modules are fixed words (graceui, the observer, the stand-in, eldennet, the game, dxgi, d3d12, d3d12core, dinput, xinput, user32, ntdll, kernel, system, other; null and unknown), keeping its rule; `graceui-faults.txt` beside it names each by its file name. Each site (the instruction and the frame above it) is written once, at most 64 a run. The handler allocates nothing and takes no lock (the faulting thread may hold the heap's or the loader's), reading a module table the observer's thread refreshes twice a second and writing from its stack; a fault inside it is not recorded again. `report.py` lists the faults and judges none, since a game's own first-chance faults are no failure. `graceui.dll` is unchanged | A minidump (it carries memory, and a full one never leaves the player's PC, EldenNet's O71); an unhandled-exception filter (the game's own may come first, and the launcher's watcher saw nothing) | The return address at a null call names the caller the crash event cannot; one run then says whose pointer it was | D175, D230; EldenNet's spike package case A1; the spike scenarios |

While either source has given motion within the last second, `WM_MOUSEMOVE`'s position is left out; with neither (a game with a free cursor, a tablet's absolute input), the position is used as before. The harness's menu-recentred scenario has a game that recentres its cursor every frame and reads raw mouse input: the pointer moves by the counts sent, stays where it was moved, and stops at the client area's edge (and stays at the centre with the old behaviour) | A hook on SetCursorPos or ClipCursor (a new target, the owner's approval, and the game's camera would then drift); registering raw input for the game's window | It uses only what GraceUI already sees, and the pointer goes where the hand moves | §5.2, D130, D176, D102; EldenNet's spike package case A5 |

A test build now makes every block of graceui.dll's own through one guard (`src/grace-guard`, pure, its suite on Linux under the sanitizers): its operator new and delete and Dear ImGui's allocator. Each block has a 64-byte header (a seal over its size and number, the three frames of the code that made it, the live list's links), 16 known bytes before and after it; a freed block is filled with another and kept in a quarantine of 4096 blocks and 64 MiB (a block over 1 MiB goes back at once; at most 64 KiB of each is filled). A block is checked at its free, as it leaves the quarantine, and every 5 s on GraceUI's thread (`GRACEUI_TEST_HEAP_CHECK_MS`, 100 to 600000); a damaged block is never freed. The guard's lock is held only while its lists and counts change: a block is checked, filled and given back to malloc in no list, a block between its free and the quarantine sealed as such, so that a second free meanwhile is still found. The maker's frames are found from operator new's own return address in the stack, so that neither inlining nor a tail call shifts them. Each state line carries the guard's counts; report.py lists them and the breach, judging neither. ## 14. Sources

- **The owner's request** (2026-11-07): a separate repository named GraceUI; a layout like SkyrimNet's dashboard; the
  appearance of SeverActions' menu (built on Magelight UI); Elden Ring tabs and settings; earlier the same day, a main
  EldenNet UI on a hotkey such as F5. Later the same day, on the first proposal: the layout was not yet like
  SkyrimNet's, and EldenNet is a framework, not a game plugin, so the menu should hold categories of an AI framework,
  taken from how SkyrimNet groups them, with what Elden Ring's version needs (D38, D39).
- **EldenNet's design** (branch `design/v1-proposal`): DESIGN §1.2, §1.3, §3.4, §4.4, §6.4, §7.6, §8.1, §9, §10,
  §11.2–§11.5, §12, §13, §16.2, §19 (O1, O8, O9, O11, O16, O17, O18, O20, O25, O28–O43, O45, O46, O60), §7.8; ARCH
  §1.2, §2.3, §2.4, §11.2, §11.5, §11.6, §12.4; NPC §5.4, §9.1, §10.1, §10.6, §10.7; MEM §9.5, §12.8; ER02 §7.1; SN01
  §6, §9, §14; WHY§102; and the C++ build of EldenNet's engine (its CMake project and `THIRD_PARTY.md`), followed by
  D106.
- **GraceUI's research**: [ML](research/magelight.md), [LOOK](research/severactions-look.md),
  [IA](research/information-architecture.md), [REN](research/renderers.md), [SND](research/skyrimnet-dashboard.md)
